# Chapter 00 The Fall of the Abbey

* **The Narwell Caravan:** Hired by the merchants of Narwell, the party escorted a small merchant caravan into the Gnarley Forest, using the wagons as bait to draw out the highly organized brigands plaguing the region.  
* **The Ogre's Toll:** Deep in the forest, the caravan's path was blocked by a massive ogre demanding payment for safe passage. Rather than engaging the 59-hit-point brute in a draining melee, the party successfully bypassed the threat through a combination of copper bribes and intimidation.  
* **Jijohn the Tinkerer:** The group crossed paths with an eccentric, wizened gnome tinkerer named Jijohn (or "Gee John"). The gnome offered cryptic warnings about the organized forces backing the bandits, recounted the crushing of the Temple of Elemental Evil ten years prior, and advised the party to seek out Burne and Rufus upon reaching Hommlet.  
* **Infiltrating the Abbey:** Leaving the caravan to strike at the root of the threat, the party located the bandit stronghold—the ruined Abbey of Allitur. The rogues (Zephyr and Sequoia) and the masked monk (Zinia) executed a flawless, stealth-based assault on the lower levels, slaughtering the unsuspecting bandits in a flurry of sneak attacks before the alarm could be raised.  
* **The Orc Cook's Confession:** Amidst the carnage in the kitchen, the party cornered the orc cook, Urot. Terrified by the party's ruthless efficiency, she confessed that the bandit leader, Snej (referred to as "Snige"), was no mere outlaw, but an operative receiving resources from a "big shot" within the Temple of Evil: Canon Belsornig.  
* **The Desecrated Chapel & The Silver Dragon:** In the ruined chapel of the Abbey, amidst defaced frescos of the god Allitur, the party discovered a captive baby silver dragon named Airgid. Chained to a statue, the ravenous hatchling explained her mother was in the Lortmil Mountains. The party agreed to escort her to Hommlet to plan a reunion.  
* **Clearing the Ruins:** The party dispatched a giant spider lurking in the collapsed roof of a guest room in mere seconds, securing the area for a brief rest.  
* **The Bandit Hoard:** Raiding the bandits' hidden cache, the party liberated a substantial hoard of treasure, claiming 123 gold pieces, *dust of disappearance*, and a *bag of holding* to add to their growing arsenal


# Chapter 01 And so it begins

Date: Planting 1 through 15, 579 

The heroes arrived in Hommlet, a small village at the edge of Verbobonc’s territory near the Gnarley Forest. They had protected a caravan from Narwell that went through the forest. The journey through the forest leads them to discover brigands. One of those Brigands worked for Canon Belsoring, a leader of the Temple of Water. 

Upon arrival, they visited the Inn of the Welcome Wench, met Spugnoir Balkof and Furnok, the fabulous rogue, and encountered the innkeeper Osler Goodingoat. The group also visited the church of St. Cuthbert, where the cleric was offered to join the group. They visited the traders Rannos and Gremag, who convinced them to hire men-at-arms for protection. They visited the leatherworker who would sell all his goods through the traders. The group is currently at the Inn of the Welcome Wench.

# Chapter 02 Detailed Summary 

## Date: Planting 15, 579 

Zinia, always the master spy, decided that they needed information. The best way to get it was to talk to folks in a Tavern looking for someone to buy them a drink. 

Looking around, he noticed that these were all common folk, farmers, traders, and passers-by. 

It was midday, and these people had no business or chores. 

Precisely the kind of people who would be most interested in telling him what they knew. One talked about a Black Jay. He was a mysterious and forlorn herdsman in league with local bandits. This mysterious man would tip the bandits off. 

Zinia thinks, “Ah, so these folks don’t know the relationship to the Temple. Good.” 

The following person, a stout woman of indeterminate middle age, mutters that Y’dey, the head cleric of the village, has mysteriously disappeared. The priest, Terjon, is awfully closed-mouthed about the situation. She best that he played no small role in her disappearance as he is in charge now.

Zinnia reflects, “Distrust of central authority, and prone to conspiratorial thinking, Good.” 

Finally, the last one reveals his take on the local gendarmerie: “Burne and Rufus are nothing but lackeys for the Crown of Verbobonc. Once they complete their castle (“Burne’s Folly” is what I would call it), they will reveal their true motivations, and we will be under the thumb of Verbobonc’s Tyrannical Rule.” 

Zinnia connects the dots and explains to his companions, “The locals see the threat of being assimilated by Verbobonc as bigger than the temple. It’s probably related to their allegiance to the god Beory and Verbobonc’s theocracy. Verbobonc is committed to St. Cuthbert, and that god is committed to proselytizing and eliminating through conversion anyone who doesn’t at least acknowledge the importance of St. Cuthbert.”  

Zinia tells the group that the locals know nothing more of value. So, Zephyr leads the group to Jaroo, a local druid and follower of Beory. 

The grove is a place of worship. The caretaker has neatly pruned the trees, and the grass is well-tended. A carefully placed line of bluish stones sets off the path to a rock cairn where flowers, nuts, berries, and leaf garlands are placed. A small path leads beyond the shrine to a low-roofed wooden building placed under the great boughs of the central oaks of the thicket.

Zephyr shouts, and soon, an older man in a robe with a hood over his head and a flowing beard appears. 

“Have you come to contribute?” says the man, pointing to a collection box. 

A donation is made, and the robed man thanks them. 

“I am Jaroo, the keeper of this blessed grove.” 

Zephyr starts to ask him questions about what’s happening, but he notices that Jaroo looks at him with unmistakable disgust. 

Zephyr immediately realizes that Jaroo is a druid who thinks that Tieflings are outside the natural order and have no place in the prime material plane. He shrugs and lets Sequoia speak.

From the conversation, he learns that Jaroo was sent here to observe the events after the defeat of the Temple of Elemental Evil. Jaroo is upset that Verbobonc isn’t interested in only protecting Hommlet but is also committed to absorbing the faithful into its Church. 

Jaroo distrusts the intentions of Burne and Rufus, and Terjon's goals.   
When Sequoia tells him of the threats they encountered, Jaroo tells them of the Moathoase. 

“The mouthouse was once the outpost of the Temple of Elemental Evil, its watchtower an advance base for raids, looting, and destruction. From this area, servants of the Temple were to bring the Village of Hommlet and all the lands around it into subjection. The temple enslaved the  conquered folk, and then they were forced to construct yet another fortress further west, spreading the evil power of the Temple in ever-growing rings to encompass all of the lands around its base.”

Jaroo pauses, “This ended with the Battle of Emiry Meadows.” 

He thinks some more and continues, “ The circles of outposts that you and your friends have uncovered are too early, similar to the plots of the original temple, to be a coincidence.”

Jaroo then turns to them, “You must go and investigate the Moathouse. And when you have found what you are looking for, you must tell me and me alone. Do not tell anyone else because the agents of the Temple may even be here. As for Burne and Rufus, they may use whatever information they glean to restrict our freedoms.” 

From there, Sequoia leads the group to Burne and Rufus. Burne and Rufus are the local leaders and representatives of Verbobonc tasked with building a castle. Sequoia is confused about the purpose of such a vast military castle in a place like Homlet. To the south is the Gnarley forest. Past the forest is the Wild Coast to the southeast and the Kingdom of Celene to the southwest. Neither are serious threats. The theory that Jaroo has is that it’s about putting Hommlet entirely under the thumb of the Archibishop of Verbobonc, and a way to extend his power looks pretty reasonable. 

As he reaches the edge of the village, he notices the tower, the only part that has been built. 

This structure is some 55 feet tall, a smaller tower rising inside the greater at about 35 feet above the ground. Its entrance is accessible only by going up a curving flight of stone stairs, which terminate in a landing about 10 feet above the ground. The outer door of the tower lowers to form a bridge to the stone landing. There are several arrow slits around the tower, and it has a splay around the base to about 6 feet in height. The lower and upper battlements are machicolated, and the merlons are pierced for archers. Two men-at-arms watch from its roof.

The guard at the door asks them for their business. 

“We came in with the latest caravan from Narwell and have news about brigands.” 

The guard coughs and lets them in. 

Shuttered arrow slits flank the door to the room, and two murder holes are in the ceiling. Flanking the inner portal are two fireplaces, where most of the cooking is done by two to three servants. The lords of the place sit in judgment either here or at a table in the curved alcove. Trestle tables can be extended down and across to accommodate 40 people during a feast or revel. Several comfortable chairs are placed along the walls, and the walls themselves are hung with tapestries and decorated with shields, arms, and hunting trophies—12 shields, four long swords, two broad swords, four spears, two battle axes, and the heads and homes of various creatures. Ramps curving upwards and downwards lead to other levels.

Rufus is a powerfully built man of average height with blue eyes, a delicate nose, and golden blond hair. He is wearing white linen pants and a dark blue blouse that shows more of his chest than the Sequoia or anyone wants to see. 

Rufus introduces himself as the military leader of the area and asks Sequoia to explain everything he has learned. 

Midway through the narration, Rufus calls out to Burne, a rather youngish-looking man. Burne has a slight build, long brown hair, green eyes, and an aquiline nose. He’s wearing a mage robe with scuff marks and scratches on it. 

“Rufus, why didn’t you tell me we had guests? I would have worn the proper attire.” 

Rufus sighs, “It’s okay. You look fabulous. I am so sorry to tear you away from your experiments.” 

Sequoia immediately understands that they are an item. 

Burne asks, “So what’s so important?” 

Rufus explains that the heroes have discovered that there may be a resurgence of the Temple of Elemental Evil. 

Burne is horrified, “That’s impossible.” 

Rufus agrees and thinks momentarily, “My friends, if you were to find out that the temple was resurgent, that would be critical secret information. It would be essential that we present it to our boss in Verbobonc.”

Sequoia gets a hint but chooses to remain silent. Rufus wants him to volunteer to share information only with him, but Sequoia isn’t sure. 

Rufus sighs, “I have a better plan than relying on your good graces. I am the military commander of this area, and I am deputizing you. As my deputies, you are required to talk to me first. If you refuse my offer of being a deputy, I will have you arrested, or worse, if you explore the Moathouse. As my deputies, you are required to share any information with me, first. And then with anyone else.” 

Sequoia smiles and nods. He has seen too many people with too many conflicting agendas, each trying to control the information flow and accomplish something slightly different. Once he has information, he will decide what to share. 

### Zinia

“We need to go get our cleric, Calmer.” 

Zinia makes the long trek to the Temple of St. Cuthbert. A massive, imposing structure that is ridiculously too large for such a small village with so few souls. 

Inside, he finds Calmer, who excitedly introduces him to Terjon.

The high priest Terjon is happy to talk to Zinia alone, whom he recognizes as a recent convert. Zinia, for his part, does his best imitation of one. 

Terjon agrees with the idea that Calmer should join the group. But he does have one particular request that is so eerily familiar, 

“Whatever information you get must be shared with me and me alone. The safety of the good people of Oerth may hang in the balance.”

From there, Zinia heads to the Inn of the Welcome Wench, where he and his traveling companions secure reasonable accommodations for reasonable prices, except for Zephyr, who is a spendthrift and demands the best room. 

# Chapter 03 A Smile and a Stone

## Date: Planting 16, 579 

Zephyr

The following day, the Zephyr sits for breakfast and waits for the arrival of Calmer and the man-at-arms that Ranos Davl has promised him. 

Calmer arrives first, his armor polished and his holy symbol, the cudgel dangling from his wrist. 

Zephyr scrutinizes him. He is short, 5’2”, has copper-red hair, hazel eyes, and a flattish nose. He also has that over-eager look that religious zealots have.   

Calmer asks, “Shall we begin?” 

Zephyr begs him to sit down, and then calls Ostler to bring Calmer some food. 

Calmer orders some boiled eggs, milk and bread. 

While Calmer waits for his food, Zephyr stares at him. The silence is uncomfortable and is interrupted when a man in studded leather armor walks in. 

“There’s the Tiefling. My boss Ranos said you hired me?” 

Zephyr looks at him carefully.

“My name is Thorne. My friends call me Duke because my name sounds like a throne.” 

Zephyr takes stock of Thorne or Duke. He is a rugged man in his late 30s with a weathered face marked by a few scars from past skirmishes. His brown hair is cropped short, and his piercing green eyes are hidden beneath the brim of a weather-beaten leather cap.

Zephyr says, “Well, since we are all here, let’s go outside and wait for the rest of the crew.” 

### Zinia

The masked one had been watching Zephyr and their two new companions, trying to see if anything was off. He noticed nothing. 

He suggests that they head to the Moathouse since all that they needed to do had been done.   
![][image1]  
The journey is uneventful. He takes the road heading east towards Nulb. After a few hours of walking, he sees the track heading to the ruins. 

A scrub of thorns, thistles, weeds, and shrubs grows thickly along the edge of the track leading to the ruins. Even the track is mostly overgrown and cluttered with fallen branches and trees. Here and there, it is washed out, in other places a mire. Some game evidently still follows the pathway, however, for after a mile or sofaint traces can be seen. But even considering this, going is slow, and it takes over an hour to reach the place on horseback, or two to trudge along on foot. Considerable hacking and clearing is necessary to make the way passable, so double the time required for the first trip. After two miles, as the track turns more northerly, the land begins to sink and become boggy. Tall marsh plants grow thickly where cattails and tamaracks do not. Off to the left can be seen the jagged silhouette of the moathouse.A side path, banked high to cross over the wetland to either side, just north to the entrance of the ruin. The track here is only about 15 feet wide or so, with crumbling embankments making travel near the edge dangerous. The bogs stink. The vegetation appears dense and prolific, but somehow sickly and unhealthy, creepers and vines throwing their strangling loops over the skeletons of dead saplings and living bushes alike. The rushes and cattails rustle and bend even to a slight zephyr, and weird birdcalls, croakings, and other unwholesome sounds come faintly across the fen.

![][image2]

The Duke sets up camp just past the drawbridge that leads into the moathouse, wishing the group luck. 

Calmer is a bit insecure about this being his first adventure, so he lets Zephyr and Zinia advance first. 

Four giant frogs jump out of the pool near the bridge and attack. Although they catch the Zinia flatfooted, they miss him. When he has a chance to absorb the tactical situation, he sees that all the frogs missed. 

“The last ribbit,” he thinks. 

A few seconds later, the ground is drenched in the blood of giant frogs. 

Thurne shouts, “I can cook something nice with those legs. Tastes like chicken.” 

Zinia glares. 

He’s about to go on the drawbridge when Calmer shouts, “I think it’s time that I did what all great clerics of St. Cuthbert do, which is lead.”

Thurne shouts, “Do you want the horses?” 

Zinia watches as Calmer marches onto the drawbridge. At the last moment, the masked spy notices the bridge rotting. He’s about to shout when four more giant frogs jump onto the bridge. 

The frogs' and the party's weight causes the bridge to shatter. Zinia doesn’t fall in, but Sequoia and Calmer do. He looks around and sees Zephyr still standing on the bridge. Sequoia and Calmer have been injured from the falling debris, but so have two frogs. 

A frog chomps at Calmer and is knocked to death’s door. Zinia mutters to himself that this is not the kind of start that will ingratiate him with the clerics of St. Cuthbert. 

Zinia jumps into the water and strikes and kills one of the injured Frogs. 

From behind him, he hears another frog croak. It’s last. It is dead at Zephyr's hand.  

As Sequioa almost kills another frog, Zinia rushes to Calmer, pulls him out of the water, and patches his wound. 

When Zinia stands up, he sees the last frog is also dead from an arrow that went through the eye and into the amphibian’s skull. 

### Sequoia

“So you guys going to die before you even make it through the door,” shouts Thrune.   
Sequoia groans and drags Calmer to the campsite. Calmer mutters, “I just need a few moments.” 

Thrune can’t resist, “So are we calling it quits?” 

Calmer shouts, “No. We have come here to do a job and will do it.” Sequoia decides that he can lead, but Sequoia will observe. The door to the moathouse is hanging open on one great hinge. The other is splintered and holed but still in position, wedged and shored closed from the inside.

Past the door is a large courtyard, and to the northeast are some stairs. The stairs lead to the house portion of the moathouse. The doors at the top of the staircase are broken; one is flat on the floor, and the other is sundered. 

Zephyr shouts, “Hm, this is interesting. Some human and pack animal traffic comes this way.” 

Calmer says, “Perhaps the rogues would like to investigate and see what’s ahead? Let us get a jump on the enemy. I can stay here and guard the rear.” 

Sequoia can see the cleric’s fear and rolls his eyes \- “Zephyr, let’s go.” 

Past the door is a great hall. This was once a great audience chamber, as shown by the tattered banners and tapestries on the walls, destroyed furniture, and heaps of rotting cloth thrown into corners. Once richly appointed, it has been thoroughly searched, sacked, and despoiled. Leaves and dirt cover the floor, and cobwebs hang from walls and the ceiling above. Looking up, he sees that pieces of beams and chunks of stone poke through, indicating that the upper stories of the place are totally destroyed and likely to be impassable to anything larger than a rat. 

To the east and south are passageways, and to the northeast is a door. Sequoia examines the ground and whispers, “The foot traffic goes to the door.” 

Zephyr acknowledges him, and Sequoia maneuvers to the door to see if he can hear anything. 

When Zephyr asks if he should use a mage’s hand to open the door, Sequoia waves him off as the door is barricaded. 

A sudden bang is heard in the room just outside the door. “Zephyr has used an illusion to get the folks inside to open the door for us,” thinks Sequoia. 

The door opens, and a man in scruffy, dirty clothes with yellowing teeth peers through. He then retreats into the door, and there is muttering. Sequoia can’t understand what they are saying. He looks to Zinia and Zephyr, but they shake their heads as well. 

The two rogues and the monk co-ordinate with hand gestures. They will sneak into the room one at a time, attack, and retreat. 

Sequoia waits as Zephyr enters. He hears a body fall to the ground. As Zephyr exits, he indicates seven more left and that the leader is dead. Sequoia waits as Zinia enters. The satisfying sound of a body hitting the ground quietly precedes Zinia’s return. 

The rogue sneaks in the past through the door and sees the bandit leader dead on the ground, pooling in blood in the middle of the room. To his left is another dead bandit. Sequoia kills one with his shortswords and then leaves the chamber. 

The bandits recover from the shock of the surprise attack and start screaming that they have to get out of there. Three of the bandits escape to the south through an arrow slit that has been enlarged to allow easy retreat.

Sequoia kills one more of the bandits and then stands in front of the exit, blocking it. 

He turns to Zinia, “Keep him alive. We want to interrogate him.” 

Zinia knocks him out with a flurry of blows. 

Sequoia stands up, and just then, Calmer walks in and says, “I think it’s important that we callto  calm down.” 

The rogue stares at the cleric, wondering if that was intentional; the cleric's obvious glee makes it clear that it was. 

He groans. 

Sequoia then wakes up the bandit. The poor man wakes up, clears his eyes, and is shocked by a Tiefling, a cleric of St. Cuthbert, and Sequoia staring at him, their weapons covered in the blood of his gang. It’s too much, and the bandit cries. 

Under questioning, the bandit explains that he didn’t want to be a bandit, but there was this property that he had to sell, then there were the gambling debts and the drinking, and well, it just went downhill from there. 

He explains, “The Moathouse was a good hideout because nobody wanted to come here anymore. The Temple was dead, and adventurers picked this place clean, so nobody came here.” 

“You were working for the Temple,” Sequoia explains coldly and flatly. 

The Bandit, whose name is Voss, starts crying even more. Sequoia is baffled, and Calmer explains that membership in the cult is punishable by death.

Out of the corner of Sequoia’s eye, he sees a flat river rock inscribed with an eye and a tear. “What’s this?” 

Voss then starts to cry hysterically and in panic. Calmer offers to help, but apparently, Calmer’s idea of calming a man terrified of being killed by an Evil Cult is to explain to him what the Church of St. Cuthbert does to its enemies. 

Sequoia pushes the cleric away and sits down next to Voss. He says, “Look, I know you are just a desperate man caught up in something bigger than themselves. And I really don’t need to hand you over to the authorities. I just need to know what this symbol is.” 

Voss looks at him, “It’s what the big boss gave the boss who gave it to us. It meant we belonged.” 

Zephyr groans, “He’s too low down the totem pole to know anything useful.” 

# Chapter 04 Recruitment, Loaded Dice, and Midnight Whispers

### Date: Planting 16, 579 

The stone's strange markings suggest elemental evil, and after a confession from one of the bandits, the group decides to return to Hommlett. 

A few hours later, the heroes can see “The Tower” in the distance and wonder where to go with their collected information. Everyone in town wants to be told first, 

Calmer insists, “We must go to the Prefect and inform him of what has happened\!” 

Sequoia, Zinia, and Zephyr agree. Of all the factions, at least this one had skin in the game. 

As they trudge through the village, the locals notice them and quietly discuss why the adventurers are back. 

Once inside the temple of St. Cuthbert, Calmer excitedly explains to Prefect Terjon the proof that he and his companions have found.

The Prefect examines the stone that Calmer has produced, rubs it between his fingers, weighs it, and finally says in disgust, “You bring me a painted rock. You had a prisoner I could have questioned; now, all I have is your word. Go find me some actionable information, not this trinket and your words.” 

Calmer is shocked.

Zinia asks for the rock, but Terjon surprisingly says, “I would like to keep this worthless rock.” 

Zephyr is intrigued by Terjon wanting to hold onto a worthless rock. He decides that he doesn’t wish Terjon to keep it. He smiles sweetly, “If this rock is so worthless, why keep it?” 

Terjon winces as if something painful has moved in his stomach, and then he tosses the rock to Zephyr. Terjon mutters as it flies through the air, “You’re right. It is worthless.” 

Zephyr, the stone in his fist, exits the temple. As his eyes adjust to the sunlight, he notices two militiamen. They are local militia members and have orders to bring the four to Burne and Refus, who are currently drinking at the Inn of the Welcome Wench. 

The tiefling smiles, and soon, the entire group follows the militiamen. 

At the Inn, in the main hall, Burne and Refus are sitting at the table, drinking. 

For some reason, Calmer bows out and says he must return to the Temple to complete some prayers. Before he leaves, he asks, “Tomorrow at the same time?”   
The group sits at the table with the two men. Once comfortable, Rufus asks, “What did you find?”

Zephyr hands over the rock.

Two scrutinize the rock, and finally, Burne mutters, “This is it?” 

Zephyr is not surprised and shares that the stone was found on the body of a bandit. 

Burne and Rufus’ reaction is a surprise. They suddenly become thoughtful, and Rufus whispers, “We may have a job for you.  I was recently examining the blueprints for the keep and found them missing. My architect had no idea where they went. When I rechecked them later, they had returned, but there were traces of charcoal dust on them. I suspect that someone has copied our blueprints. I have moved the plans into a more private part of the tower, but I guess it's a saboteur.  Until you brought me this rock, I was not convinced this was worth worrying about. But now, now I am concerned. The thief must have worked with the bandits. I am convinced it is one of the workers in the encampments. You must go and find the thief.” 

Burne stretches and says, “And keep this quiet. We don’t want to tell anyone.” 

Zephyr thinks, “Other than half the village.” And then he says, “Of course.” 

The two men then thank Zephyr and leave with their guards. 

As Zephyr, Zinia, and Sequoia enjoy their drink, Ostler, the innkeeper, sits at the table \- “I see Burne and Rufus like you.” 

“Not sure I would call it like.” 

“I also noticed that you didn’t talk to the Druid. Does the Druid not like you?” 

“Yes, he does,” insists Zephyr. 

“I’ll believe it when he says so, which will happen after you talk to him about whatever you talked to Burne, Rufus, and Terjon about.” 

Zinia gets the point and gets up, heading to the door. Ostler grabs him by the arm, “So that you know; adventurers have a minimum three-night stay at the Inn. And as a matter of policy, any goods left behind are sold after three days.” 

Like all the other town leaders, he is disappointed when Jaroo is presented with the stone and confession.

“You must go deeper into the moathouse and find more evidence,” he admonishes. 

Zephyr sighs and heads out of the grove. As he leaves, Jaroo shouts, “Who else have you spoken to?” 

Zinia realizes this town has no secrets, and he tells the truth. 

Jaroo is pleased and offers them the blessing of Beory and a healing potion. 

“We should go talk to the village elder,” mutters Zinia. As an expert on local politics, he understands the importance of getting local leaders on board. 

![][image3]

And so Zinia leads them to a place with an eight-foot-high stone wall and a heavy gate. It is the residence of some well-to-do folk and easily defensible in times of trouble.

They open the gate and step inside. Looking around, Zinia quickly discovers that the compound has five families: the village elder and his wife, the four sons and their wives. A gaggle of children of different ages is running around. 

He also notes that there are some servants here as well.

For his part, Sequoia notices how two sons carefully groom the horses. He thinks that they must be very proud of their possessions. 

Just then, one of the wives approaches the trio \- “The elder is waiting for you.” 

Past the central doorway, there is a large seating area with several comfortable chairs, a divan, several colorful rugs, and a small table. Standing in the middle of the room is an older man with white hair who looks reasonably fit and whose eyes show uncommon wisdom. 

“My name is Kenter, although folks call me Elder Kenter. Please sit.” 

As the trio sits, one of his daughters brings out some glasses and a carafe of wine and pours. 

When the Elder gets his glass, he coughs and toasts, “The Blessings of Beory.” 

After taking a sip, he turns to the trio and says in a grandfatherly voice, “It is good that you came here. My second son, Hawthorne, saw you come from the moathouse. He followed you and saw you visited Jaroo, Terjon, Burne, and Refus.” 

Sequoia is tempted to protest, but Kenter continues, “Don’t worry, whenever anyone does something out of the ordinary, Hawthorne’s natural curiosity is to learn. And it’s good that he did. The people you spoke to are on the council. And, unfortunately, they chose not to have you present to the council, where all could learn simultaneously.” 

There is a slight pause, and he continues: “The council members are Jaroo, Rufus, Burne, and Ostler, whom you have already met, as well as Captain Zeb Fletcher, father of Elmo and Mytch the Miller.” 

The trio mutely stares, so he finishes, " The bandit activity surge is alarming. It's not just an economic issue; it's a threat to the safety and stability of our village. We must address it with utmost urgency. Burne and Rufus have shown remarkable leadership and strategic acumen. Their efforts in organizing defenses and training our militia have been crucial in our response to the bandit threat. Our unified leadership is our greatest strength. By working together, Burne, Rufus, Captain Fletcher, and I can coordinate our efforts and ensure a comprehensive response to the bandit menace.” 

Sequoia drinks. 

The Elder concludes, “Therefore, going forward, I expect you all to come here first.” 

They all nod and leave politely after sampling some of the treats that Kenter’s wife, Valsenora, brings to the table. 

“Well, that was interesting,” mutters Zinia. 

From there, the trio heads to the Inn of the Welcome Wench, where Ostler, obviously informed of the visit to Jaroo and the blessing, greets the trio at the door.   
From there, he ushers them into the kitchen, explaining that his job offer is to prove that Furnoc is a crook who steals from the Inn’s guests. 

To sweeten the pot, he offers free rooms for three nights. 

Zephyr smiles, “No problem.” 

Unfortunately, it is a problem, as his attempt at pick-pocketing Furnok the Fabulous fails. Seeing his opportunity lost, he creates a distraction for Sequoia or Zenia to try their luck. So he falls on the table that Furnok is sitting on, causing the table to tip over. 

Zinia moves quickly and grabs the dice. As he picks them up, he realizes they are loaded—“The proof I needed\!” 

Ostler confronts Furnok, who rejects the accusations. Things start to escalate when a burly, muscular man sitting at another table shouts, “Get out of here, Furnok, or I’ll have to call the Captain, and he won’t be as kindly as Ostler is.” 

Sequoia realizes that this is Elmo. He also smells the stench of booze on him: “A drunk.” 

And yet, Sequoia and the rest of the trio find themselves sitting around Elmo’s table. They quickly learn that Elmo is an adventurer and a ranger by profession. 

Zephyr smiles, “There is a monster in the Gnarley forest, and we will need someone to help us find it.” 

Elmo mutters, “I am your person\!” 

While this witty dialogue is going on with Elmo, Zinia carefully listens to the conversations and talks amongst the patrons. Overhearing one pair, he learns  that a farmer's daughter, Lysia, is itching to explore the ruined boathouse, but she needs some equipment and a few competent companions.” The person hearing the tale turns to his companion and replies, “ I hope she can swing a sword better than she can plow a field.” 

From another person, he hears, “ There is a secret passage below the inn that leads to an extensive set of four lost chambers of a long-dead wizard.” 

Zephyr is tempted to sneak around and see what’s happening inside the kitchen until Sequoia suggests they talk to Spugnoir. 

“You must be joking\! The only thing under this inn is stinky food\!” Spugnoir pauses. “But I know the location of a lost wizard laboratory under Emiry Meadows. I am offering to split the treasures as long as I get the first crack at any arcane scrolls we find.” 

The trio stares at him, confused. 

“I would like to go adventuring with you and complete your quartet. If you would have me?” 

Sequoia laughs, “Somehow, you are very familiar.” He then pauses, “Sure.” 

“Tomorrow then, as it is quite late\!” 

After dinner, the trio discusses their plans for the next day when a young man walks up to them and whispers, “Rumor has it that you are the right people to go to if there is a problem.” 

“Yes?” says Sequoia

“My name’s Mickey,” the boy says. The Trio stares at him, confused; the boy realizes they don’t know him and says, “I am the son of Virgil Tamen, the Braumeister.” Still, there is silence. The boy then plunges ahead, “ I've seen a couple of cloaked people lurking behind the brewery at around midnight.

Yeah, I, I'd like, could, could you guys look into it? 'cause I'm worried, I mean, I, I, you know, I, the rumors around the village is going that the temple isn't as dead as everybody thought. And so maybe, those are, uh, spies.” 

The Trio stares at each other and then wordlessly agrees to look into this potential threat. 

Mickey smiles and leaves, relieved. 

Towards midnight, Zephyr, Zinia, and Sequoia climb outside their room window and drop to the ground. The village is eerily quiet, with none of the noise of the day. In particular, Sequoia notices how much noise the castle construction makes. 

The village has no lights, making the journey to the brewery much easier. Zinia and Sequoia take the position to watch anyone arriving or leaving, and Zephyr plans to sneak up on the spies. 

At around midnight, Mickey’s spies show up. Zephyr quickly advances on them, his sword in his right hand, and puts the blade to the throat of the smaller of the two—the spy shrieks. 

Before he can do anything, the other spy starts to cry. 

Zinia and Sequoia come rushing out of their hiding spots; weapons bared and somewhat confused. 

Zephyr uses all his tiefling fierceness to force the two squealing teenagers to be quiet. And then he asks, “What’s going on?”   
The girl introduces herself as Jira, and the boy as Marek. They explain that Jakub is a member of the old faith and won’t let Jira be with Marek, whose father is Tomlyn, the tailor and a member of the Church of St. Cuthbert. 

Zinia snarls and tells them to go away. 

As they leave, Jira pleads with Zinia to help them and talk to their parents \- “You’re heroes, and they’ll listen\!” 

Sequoia struggles to sleep as he thinks about Zephyr looking confused and the two teenagers squealing. 

### Date: Planting 17, 579 

The following day, the group decides to return the silver dragon to its home. The dragon Airgid has been straining the budget over the last few days, and the group has increasingly been worried that it might decide to feed on some horses.   
![][image4]

The dragon explains that its home is near the Lotrimil mountains. Ostler explains that that’s to the west through the Kron Hills. He also tells them that it’s a pretty safe journey. 

“The fastest way is to head south and turn north into the Greenway Valley. A reasonably well-traveled road passes small gnomish mining towns.” 

The journey takes them past the moat house, and while the place calls to them for a moment, they have another job right now. 

A little before they arrive at Tulvar, after a day of hard traveling, the dragon suddenly shouts, “HOME\!” 

Airgid takes off and starts to fly west. As she leaves, she shouts, “Don’t worry, my mommy will reward you\! I KNOW SHE WILL\! THANK YOU\!” 

Session 5

### Date: Planting 18, 579 

The heroes are in Hommlet at the Inn of the Welcome Wench, and Spugnoir and Calmer stare at the heroes. Both had expected to go on an adventure the previous day and discovered that the heroes had left without them. 

Zephyr looks at them and turns to Spugnoir, “It’s Calmer. We need a healer for the Moathouse.” 

Spugnoir has that crushed look of someone who is too used to not being picked for the team. He smiles, “Very well.” 

Outside of the Inn is the Duke. 

“So we heading out?” 

A little bit later, they are once again at the moathouse. 

The Duke sets up camp.

“I hope I see you again.” 

Calmer gulps.

Inside the courtyard, Calmer asks, “What do we want to check out?” 

Zinia points to the tower that they ignored last time.

The two rogues peer inside. The upper portion of the tower has collapsed, and the interior is dark. Looking at the floor, Sequoia and Zephyr notice a faint gleam of coins. 

As they reach for the coin, a giant wolf spider attempts to turn them into its next meal. But it is overmatched by the two rogues and the monk. 

They find an ivory box about the size of a large book among the treasures. 

With his allies following, Sequoia crosses the courtyard, enters the great hall, and turns west into a passageway with two doors on the south and one to the north. The passageway ends at the door. 

The first door to Sequoia’s left opens into a trophy room. Only a few mangy pelts, stuffed heads, and shattered antlers remain. Zinia, who enters the room, looks more carefully and realizes that some of the trophies are the heads of dwarves, humans, and elves \- “Enemies of the temple,” he mutters..” 

The next door on the southern wall opens into the domicile of the majordomo of the castle. Zephyr sighs; it’s stripped of anything valuable. 

From there, Zephyr opens the westernmost door, where the hallway ends. Much like the other parts of the castle, this room is partially collapsed. The remains of cots and plain wooden chests convince the rogue that this was once a barracks room for castle guards—undoubtedly the human sort.

Zephyr notices an intact chest at the south end of the room. As he approaches it, he notices a giant lizard just in time. The lizard was using the chest as a lure. This time, the poor creature died. 

Sequoia leads the group past the last door in the hallway and into a room with a stairway leading down. 

It also has a swarm of rats. Rats that are quickly killed. 

Calmer turns to the group, “I suggest we finish exploring the ruins of the moathouse. Don’t want any nasty surprises to follow us.” 

Sequoia agrees. They cross the wrecked great hall and head down the southern passageway, which is 70 feet long and has three doors. The hallway runs north-south. 

The first door the rogue opens reveals an old conference room chamber.  He sniffs and shuts the door, “Nothing to be found.”

Sequoia mutters, “And for door number two,” as he opens the next door on the western wall. And in this chamber, he finds what must have been a bed chamber for some lowly official.   
Across the hallway, he sees another door and excitedly opens it. He looks inside, and although the room is in shambles and things are missing, whatever remains still appears valuable. He enters carefully, and as he picks up the treasures, he realizes they are worthless. 

He looks around and mutters, “A salon for meetings?” 

Leaving the salon and reentering the hallway, he walks into the large chamber at the end of the hallway. Much like other parts of the moathouse, this room is empty of anything valuable. Unlike other rooms, this room has a giant poisonous snake that attempts to strike. It fails. 

Soon, like the other nuisance monsters the group has faced, it is dead. 

# Chapter 05  

Events were not recorded.

# Chapter 06 Repast for an Ogre

## Date: Planting 18, 579 

### Calmer

I will boldly lead the group down into the lower level of the moathouse. 

There is no doubt that some evil powers are here. And I will defeat them\! 

But first, a brief rest. It’s essential to rest when facing monsters. Back in Verbobonc, one of the trainers argued that rest, not activity, builds strength. Rest allows the muscles to learn the lessons of what has happened. 

The Duke is, predictably, less than friendly. He is a bit full of himself and mocks me as I stretch. 

It’s essential to engage the muscles after a workout.

And now we are standing at the threshold of the lower levels of the moathouse. A staircase is next to the great hall, where we faced brigands who may or may not have been aligned with the Temple of Elemental Evil. 

I insist that I must lead. My experience and those innate attributes of wisdom and perception make me the right person to be in front. 

Zephyr walks with me. Zinia and Sequoia are behind.

And it’s a good thing I was in front because I noticed, just in time, the blob of green slime hanging over the arch at the bottom of the stairwell. 

### Zephyr

Calmer brags about how he can see everything. And then a blob of green slime lands on him. And he starts squealing. 

Calmer, he should be Louder.

Using some wood I happened to be carrying on me, I removed the slime from his armor. Unfortunately, the slime ate through it. 

Calmer is now whimpering at the fact that his beautiful, shiny armor has been destroyed. 

“Grow up,” I tell him. And then we walk through the archway. 

Looking around, this place seems as abandoned as the upper layers. There are a few torn sacks, broken barrels, and shattered weapons racks. This was once a storage place and armory for the fortress. 

And there’s no treasure.

All I can see are heaps of worthless rubbles and broken containers at the western end of the room. 

“Junk,” I whisper. 

Calmer has finally calmed down. 

### Sequoia 

If the west has nothing but junk, to the east, we must go. 

And there is a door.

Beyond the door is a littered storeroom. 

That’s interesting. The door hinges are greased. For a place that should be abandoned, that’s odd. 

I then overhear a bang. I pull my swords, ready to strike, and realize it’s the cleric. 

I want to kill him. If there is anything past the door to the east, it heard us. 

“I am Lubash\!”

Standing at the now-open eastern door is an ogre. 

“You are not members of the temple\! You are food\!” 

And it swings it’s club. 

And boy do Ogre clubs hurt. 

### Zinnia

This amateur-hour infiltration of the temple has got to stop. We have a cleric who can’t keep his feet on the ground, and instead of using some kind of disguise, we just march around like the kind of intruders anyone who sees here will attack first and speak with the dead later. 

But first, we need to kill this Ogre.   

My fists pummel the creature. Zephyr and Squoia’s blades cut him to size, but not before Calmer almost dies again. 

With the dead carcass lying in the doorway, I enter carefully into the room beyond. 

That’s interesting. 

This place looks lived in. And there in the corner is where the Ogre lived. 

I look inside the Ogre’s nest and discover a magical cloak of elvenkind. 

“Now that’s even more interesting. Who is the elf that lost this?” 

An Ogre needs a lot of food. And so someone has been providing it with food. 

And why is that door to the south so heavily barred? 

Calmer, who has recovered from being attacked by an ogre, and I clear the door. 

I carefully open it with Zephyr and Sequoia by my side. We tell Calmer to stand back. I would tell him to stand all-the-way back to Hommlet, but we need a healer. 

“Who are you?” says a tired and frightened voice. 

 “Who are you?” I ask. 

The mystery of the Ogre’s food is unraveled. And it’s a pretty grisly story. 

Two of the prisoners are merchants. They are wearing the traveling clothes of the rich, now covered in dirt, blood, and excrement. 

One of them, wearing a strange purple robe, says, “My name is Derrick, and this is Florian. We’re originally from Norwell. Brigands captured us along the road to Hommlet. Those captured alive were brought here as a repast for the Ogre. There were six of us. Every day, the Ogre would come in and take one of us. First, there was Martin, Damien, Eltograph the Elf, and…” 

Damn it, he’s crying. 

“I can help?” shouts Calmer. And I think, the last time he helped, he started berating someone about their life choices. 

“No, I think I can handle this.” 

The merchants make it clear they have no other helpful information but promise a reward if brought back to Hommlet. 

As for the other prisoner, he’s way more interesting. He’s a gnome and a spy. 

“My name is Jarran. I was sent out here by the merchants of Lotrimil to investigate the brigands attacking this area. I didn’t expect to get caught and put in this pantry room. I have a mission. And I need to get back to it. Although, if you need another sword and promise to keep exploring, I could stick around.” 

I don’t trust other spies. 

“No, I don’t think that’s necessary. Let’s get you all outside.” 

### The Duke

They’re still alive. And they are bringing a bunch of people. This is interesting. And these people look like they were trapped in the moathouse. That’s very interesting. 

“Don’t you worry, team. I’ll make sure to guard them. When you come back, for good, we can all take them back to Hommlet.”

### Sequoia

“Let’s check that hallway to the south we ignored last time, guys.”

The hallway is 50 feet long and runs north-south. Three pillars are 20 feet apart, and a passageway leads east. 

Five doors are on the western wall. They look like they haven’t been opened in a long time. Something like prison doors designed to keep people in. Well, those doors are so rotten that they won’t keep anyone in anymore. 

Okay, let’s be careful here. 

“Zombies\!” 

Everyone says they are saying “Brains\!” but that’s not true. They aren’t saying anything. But what happens is the sound that escapes from their decaying vocal cords sounds like “nsazsa,” and everyone thinks they are saying Brains\!” 

I wonder if Calmer has any power over the undead\! And yes, he does\! 

They are turned. We have about a minute to check out what’s here. 

And that’s when I hear it.

“Brains\!”

Remember those doors that won’t keep anyone inside? Well, they aren’t keeping those zombies inside the cell. 

Okay, we can handle this. Two zombies shouldn’t be a problem. 

Except, what exactly is in the other cells?

I can worry about that later. 

“Brains\!”

There were more zombies in that cell.

That’s okay. We can handle it. 

And then I hear the sound again. 

“Brains\!” 

MORE ZOMBIES? 

That’s when Zephyr shouts, or is it Zinia? Honestly, I can’t tell. 

“The other three doors also have zombies\!” 

“Time to get out of here,” I shout. Zephyr’s like, “We can take them\!” 

I look at him and think, the next time, there will be 13 zombies to kill…

But he’s not that foolish. And runs for the archway and the stairs that go up. 

A few minutes later, we’re standing in front of the Duke, who is giving out food and talking to the merchants. The gnome is still sitting there and hands us a ring. 

# Session 7	Repast for the Ogre 

## Date: Planting 18, 579 

### Sequoia

The road back to Hommlet is quiet.  The gnome’s promise of help later on might be helpful. 

As I see the tower on the horizon, I turn to my friends, “We should go to the Druid first.” 

Calmer looks at me, confused, “But we have an obligation to the Terjon and my order\!” 

“What about the mayor?” 

I look at my tired, blood-splattered, and exhausted friends, “Because we can only trust the Druid. Everyone else is too susceptible to corruption.” 

The Druid meets us in his grove, his greybeard flowing, and listens to me, but I don’t think he is convinced. 

“You have proof of brigands. And that is important. And you have evidence that the Brigands are pretending to be members of the temple. But you have no proof.” 

I try to argue, but he interrupts me. 

“Just that they are masquerading as temple members is cause for great concern. You were wise to come to me first, but we must go to the elders. The information must be presented, but I am not sure it is time for concrete action.”

He’s right, but I'm not sure I want to present. I could say the wrong thing, and then things could get worse. I’ll ask the Druid. 

He agrees and seems flattered.

“Tomorrow then. We will meet at the Village Elder,” says Jaroo.

### Zinia

Calmer desperately wants to talk to the Cleric, so I tell him to go. He’s disappointed I won’t follow, but I can’t spend hours listening to Terjon's drone. 

So when we walk into the Inn of the Welcome Wench, the magic user Spugnoir seems particularly pleased that the cleric is not with us. 

He’s trying to be calm when he asks, “So, any interest in that tower?” 

“Tomorrow,” I tell him. 

He smiles and heads back to his table, where he seems busy writing stuff. 

As I head to my room, Ostler stops me. “I say it’s wonderful to see you back.” 

“Thanks.” 

“And your room has been paid for until the 19th.” 

“Thanks.” 

He whispers conspiratorially, “And I am very pleased that you decided to go to Jaroo before going to those interloping outsiders.” 

## Date: Planting 19, 579 

### Zinia

I come down for my breakfast, and there he is, Spugnoir.

“Are we off?” he asks. 

I so want him to go away. 

Thankfully, Sequoia intervenes, “We have an early morning meeting, Spugnoir, and then we can go.” 

A bit later, I will be in the Village Elder's house—the same room where we met with him last time. It will be one of those meetings where we spend endless time socializing. There will be food, drinking, and joking.  
I look around, and there’s Burne and Rufus, the captain of the militia, Jaroo, the village elder,  and Ostler. 

The discussion and eating take about an hour when the village elder finally calls the meeting to order. 

And then Calmer decides he wants to derail the meeting. 

“I would like to insist that the Prefect be present for these meetings.” 

This could be interesting and reveal the politics of this town, but it would take a long time. 

But Sequoia is in a rush. “I don’t have time for this. Calmer, this is not the topic of the meeting. Perhaps you can do it some other time? If you have anything relevant to this topic, you can share it with him later.” 

Calmer sighs, “Very well.” 

I listen to Jaroo repeat what we told him. He pushes the narrative that brigands are masquerading as agents of the temple than they are agents of the temple. I let it slide. 

Burne and Rufus are very dismissive and cautious. Interesting. 

Rufus says, “You have brought strong evidence of Brigand's activity, and they must be dealt with. But we have no conclusive evidence of the re-emergence of the Temple of Elemental Evil. Your information is of local importance but not national importance and certainly not worthy of disrupting the arch-cleric of Verbobonc. ” 

Burne adds, “I want to commend our heroes for their information. I am thrilled to see them working together and that our decision to deputize them has produced such excellent information.” 

At that point, the Village Elder closes the topic and lets us go unless we want to participate in the end-of-month festival planning. 

Sequoia has to ask, “What festival?” 

“Every month, we celebrate another month as a community. We wish people a happy birthday, celebrate any newborn, and mourn any deaths. You are invited, of course.” 

I was already bored and terrified when Jaroo, with a twinkle in his eye, said, " Could you help with decorating? And could you participate in the location committee?” 

Sequoia looks at me and Jaroo and says, “Gotta go\!” 

### Zephyr

I’ve seen the abyss. And let me assure you, the Abyss is not appreciably worse than that meeting. 

I was ready to demand that I be sucked into the endless blood war rather than have to listen for another minute of them talking. 

So it was quite a relief when Sequoia and Zinia ran out. Calmer and I followed, but he was slightly disappointed when we told him we were taking Spugnoir with us. He had this look of a sad puppy. 

But Spugnoir, he was happy. And while we marched to Emiry Meadows, he became very talkative. 

“There was a great battle here some years ago. I must tell you one of the most intriguing elements of that battle.”

Zinia realizes that this guy likes to be flattered and says, “Yes?” 

“There are tales that the circle of eight appeared.”

“The who?” 

“My dear Zephyr, you don’t know them? Ah yes, two rogues, a monk. Why would you? The circle of eight consists of the eight most powerful mages in the world \- Moderkainenen, Bigby, Ottiluke, and Leomund. These are legendary mages whose spells are well-known throughout the multiverse. My personal goal is to meet them.”

“Just meet? I hope you make them the circle of nine\!” 

“Thank you, Mr. Zephyr. But even I don’t have that ambition. I must understand my limits. They showed up at the battle at a crucial juncture when some artifact was being used. When the battle was over, they went to the Temple of Elemental Evil and cast powerful spells to bind some evil presence within.” 

“And?” 

“The Circle of Eight is neither good nor evil. They only care about the balance. For them to show up means that whatever was there was enough to disrupt the balance.  Something there got them to show up and do something.” 

“Do what?” 

“I don’t know what and who. All I know are stories I have heard. But who knows, perhaps one day we will all discover.” 

### Zinia

The tower that the mage promised is more of a single building. It's not quite at the Battle of Emiry Meadows but near the battle of Emiry Meadows. 

Straws are drawn, and I am going to enter first. The mage is as far back as possible. Looking inside, I see permanent markings on the floor for a summoning circle and mural. Spugnoir, peaking over my shoulder, says, “It contains the formula for a magic circle.” 

But that’s not what interests me. What concerns me is a dust and smoke mephit. 

The smoke mephit turns to us and says that if we entertain him, we won’t be attacked for entering his kingdom. 

Zephyr tries to entertain him with some song. Unfortunately, as we learn later, that song is a song that immediately induces a blood-curdling rage in smoke and dust mephits. Who would have thunk that a song about the sea and the rain and the clear day after a fire would piss them off so much? 

No matter, in a few moments, they are dead. 

I notice everyone is staring at me and at another door. I stare back. Finally, Zephyr gets the point and mutters, “I will open the door.” 

### Zephyr 

I look inside. It’s a laboratory that includes an alchemy lab and a book of magical formulae. There’s a shelf of moldering books. And a forge in the far corner. And two cultists.

They attack.

They die. 

Spugnoir walks over their bodies bleeding out on the ground and is super excited \- “Mon Dieu, this is amazing\! This is incredible.” 

He walks to the laboratory and shouts, “Two potions of gaseous form. A potion of healing. And yes, look at this, a magical dagger\!” 

There’s a bit of debate about who gets the dagger, but fair is fair, and Zinnia gets it. 

Spugnoir looks around, frustrated, and mutters, “There is a secret door somewhere.” 

I take a quick look and point it out to him. 

“Very well.” 

He looks at me as if it’s my job to go through every secret door. I look at Sequoia, who looks right back at me. I groan, “Fine.” 

Sneaking, I enter a room containing a filthy, cracked fountain that leaked onto the floor ages ago, leaving greenish stains. The fountain has recently been mended, and water is being pumped again—a cauldron, fire pit, and rotting cupboards full of moldy foods.

Someone has been here recently. 

An even smaller door is in the corner of the room. I walk quietly to it and open it. Inside is the cultist’s leader. He’s not happy to see me.

As the life escapes his body, I wonder if his reward from his infernal god will be everything he hoped for.

“Probably not.” 

I look them over to find out what cult they belong to. Well, that’s interesting; it's a spider idol. Looking it over closely, I realize it must be Lolth. A human worshipper of Lolth. 

Now, this is interesting. Lolth has been gone for a while. The scuttlebucket is that she’s trapped somewhere. 

With the cultist dead, I can look over the room. It’s a battered bedroom with gnome-scale furniture, including a bed, dresser, chest of drawers, coal bin, and pot-bellied stove. The furniture is mildewy and damaged from neglect. There are three bedrolls here. 

Spungoir is super excited, “A scroll of fog, silent image, gust of wind, misty step\! And look, a spell book with feather fall, fog cloud, mage armor, magic missile and protection from good and evil, silent image, Tenser’s floating disk, unseen servant, gust of wind, misty step, rope trick, fly gaseous form, magic circle, and sleet storm\! A virtual cornucopia of spells. With these in my repertoire, I will be much more useful to all of you\!” 

I smile. 

A bit later, we arrived in Hommlet. Spugnoir is so excited. I am, too. His spell list was useless. Maybe we will keep him around. 

Zinia asks, “So, do we rest another day or head out?”

I want to find out who copied the map. A good place to start is the workers' encampment near the fortress. 

I looked to Zinnia to see if he had any advice, but he didn’t.

So I sneak into the encampment, hoping my distinctive demonic appearance will get one of these ignorant villagers to approach me. And I am in luck: a man 30-ish years old, whose build is that of a construction worker but whose grace indicates something more, walks up to me. 

“What the hells are you doing here?” 

“Boss needs information.” 

“I gave him information. What more does he need?” 

Zephyr \- Afternoon, Planting 18, 579   
This guy isn’t going to be easily intimidated. And worse, he has opinions. 

But like most people, he’s afraid. And he’s concerned. And he’s intimidated at the sight of a Tiefling. 

Because, well, folks here think we are all hellspawn. And if you’re in league with a demonic cult, then perhaps you worry that the demonic-looking creature is working for said demon. 

But he won’t talk. 

And I’ve spooked him. And a spooked mouse will run to its house. 

## Zinnia \- Evening, Planting 18, 579 

A stakeout. 

I guess it could be worse. The masked one is hiding in a forest, waiting for a mouse to flee so we can follow it to its rabbit hole.  

I know how to stay quiet, patient, and wait. Sequoia and Zephyr do as well. Thankfully, we don’t have Calmer. He’d probably want to go and talk to the guy. 

This reminds me of the time I was. Well, that’s a secret mission. And maybe I shouldn’t think about it. 

*Crack*

Zephyr points out that the mouse is running. 

Sequoia is pursuing him from a distance as he leaves the encampment. He’s heading towards the river north of the encampment. I’ll follow Sequoia. 

The fields don’t provide much cover, but that’s okay. The mouse isn’t paying attention; he’s running scared. 

Sequoia points out that there are some people on the river. The mouse is running towards them. The allies we are looking for. Excellent. 

*Twang*

What the hell? 

The mouse just got shot by his allies, who are now running away.  

Let me examine the situation. There is a captain and four mooks. If I kill the mooks, the captain will run. If I kill the captain, the mooks will run. Mooks are easier to kill, but the captain has more information, and we need information.

# Chapter 07 A deal with Lareth, a deal with Rufus, and Sheernob

Inside the tower, past the guard, the halfling rogue is once again in the presence of the two agents of Verbobonc. Burne is wearing a leisure suit, and Rufus is wearing a white blouse, green velvet pantaloons, and a red sash. They are both sitting around the main table, obviously full.

Rufus begins, “So any evidence of the cult?” 

“They were operating as members of the fire temple.” 

“Members of the cult?” 

“Yes.”

I don’t doubt that you found evidence of cultists. But whether they are cultists of a reanimated temple or agents of some other power or government is unknown.” 

*Definitely trying to cover his ass.* 

Burne sighs, “Did you find the leader?” 

“Yes.” 

Rufus is excited, “Did you kill him?” 

Sequoia pauses. It's *probably not the correct answer to say we didn’t. But if we didn’t, why? I know—these guys are convinced this is just some agent of some other government. Burne looks at Sequoia and says, “Brilliant. I admire your deviousness. I imagine it was the masked one who devised this plan. Destroying Lareth’s enemies will weaken our enemies as well. Excellent\! What was this mission that he sent you on?”

## Planting 21, 579 Morning

### Zephyr  

The journey to Sheernobb, where the bandits were last seen, is uneventful. 

As he approaches the verdant hills of Sheernobb, the landscape changes, giving way to a charming village nestled within the lush greenery of the Kron Hills. The road narrows and winds through the dense forest, guiding him toward the heart of the gnome settlement. The air is filled with the earthy scent of the forest, mingled with the faint aroma of roasting potatoes and fresh ale.

Just as Zephyr begins to take in the quaint beauty of the village, he is halted by a group of gnome guards. They are small in stature but carry an air of authority, their eyes sharp and watchful.

“What business do you have in Sheernobb?” one of the guards demands, stepping forward. His tone is not hostile, but it carries the weight of someone who has asked this question many times.

Zephyr explains that it’s a bit of tourism \- “We were here a few weeks ago to return a dragon? You might recall. And we helped a gnomish adventurer, Knarfallan. We are also adventurers and wondered if there would be jobs we could do.” 

The guard looks at Zephyr quizzically. The gnome then notices a ring on his pinky that Knarfallan gave him. 

Once that ring is noticed, the guard’s demeanor changes instantly and is much friendlier. 

“Very well, you may enter,” he says, gesturing with a gloved hand towards the village. “But be warned, we keep a close eye on all newcomers, even friends of Knarfallan. For accommodations, we recommend the Honey Haven Tavern. It’s suited for humans and will be a comfortable place for you to stay.”

The guard looks at the Tiefling more carefully, “Or whatever you are.” 

*This again.* 

“I am a human. A victim of a curse that I have been unable to rid myself of.” 

The guard seems comfortable with the answer. 

“If you have any questions or need to speak with someone in charge, you can find Headgnome Glennddarc at the Sheernobb Moothall. It’s the largest mound in the village; you can’t miss it.”  
With that, the guards step aside, allowing Zephyr to continue his journey into Sheernobb. As he proceeds, the bustling activity of the village begins to unfold before him. Tiny burrow homes dot the hillsides, their doors intricately carved and inviting. Gnomes go about their daily routines, casting curious glances his way.

### Sequoia  

“Let’s go to the tavern.” 

Green awnings and flowering ivy tendrils adorn the outside of the squat little inn, and warm laughter burbles out through an open window. The brightly colored sign above the doorway proudly proclaims it to be The Honey Haven Inn and depicts a hand-painted bee perched on the edge of a flower. The wood panels of the exterior are weather-worn but clean—an indication of the love and care with which this place is maintained.

The gnomes of this settlement have created an inn that is perfect for people of diminutive sensibilities, though it is pretty cramped for larger humanoids. 

*Good thing I am small.* 

The benches, tables, desks, and bar have all been shaped out of Elm wood, with horsehair cushions fastened to the seat—the smell of roasting potatoes and fresh ale wafts toward Sequoia when he pushes through the door. Bright woven tapestries hang along the walls and add a touch of color to the wooden interior. The barkeep stands behind a counter to the left of the entrance, and the doorway to the kitchen opens behind them. A pair of double doors lead to the patio outback. Quinna smiles and says, “It turns out that I want to prepare a celebratory meal for the Harvest Festival. I need the meat of a rare gnarled boar that roams the gnarly forest. It's notoriously elusive and dangerous, and nobody has gotten it for me.”

Quinna squeals in delight, “ I hope to see you soon and help our small town have its festival. It will be so wonderful. It'll be so tasty, and I promise my meal will be fantastic, the likes of which you have never had.”

# Chapter 08 Investigations, and quests in Sheernob. 

## Planting 21, 579 Early Afternoon

### Zephyr 

Well, that was expensive\!

After having his pocket cleanly picked by Mari, Zephyr was looking for an opportunity to get out. 

“Let’s go explore the village and see if we can learn anything\!” 

The rest of the group stares at him. He watches as Calmer drinks the last of his mead and then tells him in as disrespectful a tone Calmer can muster: “As you wish, boss\!” 

Zephyr thinks about what to do next. The village of Sheernobb is a small town. The dominant building is what the locals call the Moot Hall, where folks go to meet. The road ends at the moot hall and splits into two branches, one going south and another north around the moot hall. The two branches converge after the moot hall on the road going out of town. The two branches of the moot hall form a circle around which most stores are located. The Tavern is on the south branch, just after the road coming into town ends. 

Zephyr remembers how a bookstore was on the northern branch. 

“To the bookstore.” 

Seeing an opportunity to explore the town more, rather than go directly to the bookstore, he decided to explore the rest of the village by continuing on the southern branch and following the road to the bookstore.  

The journey is short, and he notes the stores: “Djinibac’s Apothecary, a Tabac Shop, Fiver Woodcarver, Peony’s Mill, and would you look at that a pond, Henker’s Shop, Naglo Crystal Divine Shop, and there is Grindin Books.” 

Nestled within a small cottage, Grindin Books is instantly recognizable by the cluttered display of books and maps in its windows and a studded oak door bearing the welcoming sign, “Welcome to the Grindin Books.”   
The quaint exterior belies the wealth of knowledge contained within.

This dimly-lit bookshop has been constructed to the owner's specifications, and the owner wants to read books when not helping customers. Behind the counter, a plush chair with a clever reclining mechanism lets them put their feet on an equally plush stool. ### **Zinnia's Perspective**

After our recent skirmish with the Black Earth cultists, we secured the stolen honey from Pasmarie Clee and, crucially, found **notes indicating the lost chest was located in a monastery within the mountains**. Our return to Hommlet was punctuated by the pressing need to share this new information, though the various factions in town, with their conflicting demands, made such a task fraught.

The first stop was to Pasmarie Clee in Sheernobb, where we returned her honey and received our reward. Back in Hommlet, the immediate priority was Calmer, who had fallen in the line of duty. To revive him, I made the difficult decision to **give up a magical trident, offering it as a tithe to Terjon at the Temple of St. Cuthbert**. It was a practical move, though it still chafed, especially considering Terjon's prior dismissiveness. We also made sure to visit Jaroo, who reiterated the importance of securing the chest, noting that the Terjon (implied to be followers of St. Cuthbert like Terjon) might withhold healing magic from non-followers. Our subsequent visit to Burne and Rufus confirmed their interest in the chest, though their priorities, as usual, seemed more political than purely good.

With our immediate obligations in Hommlet fulfilled and a new lead in hand, we headed to Ostverk. Upon arrival, our first objective, as always for a spy, was to gauge the local sentiment and gather intelligence. We made our way to "The Cheap Forester," a local tavern. The tavern keeper, Robert Deakin, a gnome, insisted we purchase food before he’d talk. While consuming our mandatory meal, I keenly observed the rumors circulating:

* The local chatter confirmed that **a powerful chest had indeed been lost to bandits, and many were searching for it**. This corroborated Lareth's story and our own findings.  
* Deakin, however, was adamant that **rumors of a "big evil power" were lies, insisting they were merely bandits**. He claimed to know because he had fought in the Battle of Emiry Meadows, implying the Temple was truly destroyed.  
* He unveiled a fascinating theory: the chest's true purpose was to secure a treaty with the Kingdom of Celene. The Viscounty (Verbobonc) intended to use this alliance to **wage war on the gnomes and seize their gem mines**. Deakin, being a gnome, was clearly displeased by this, and surprisingly, was glad the alliance hadn't formed. This insight into the region's geopolitical undercurrents was invaluable.  
* It was also rumored that the elves at Caer Ostverk and Lord Denelu were willing to pay well for information on the chest's location.  
* Finally, Deakin mentioned he was related to the Clee family (our honey purveyors in Sheernobb) and even had honey mead from the Honey Tavern Inn.

The layers of conspiracy and conflicting interests in this small corner of the world continued to surprise me, even for a seasoned spy.

### **Sequoia's Perspective**

The journey to Ostverk was uneventful, a welcome change after the chaos of the Moathouse and the Kron Hills. Our primary objective was to follow the lead we’d uncovered regarding the lost chest's location in a monastery. The information from the notes of Romag to Thalsor, confirmed by an unsent note from Thalsor, was our best hope.

In Ostverk, we settled into "The Cheap Forester" tavern, a place that lived up to its name in terms of ambiance but thankfully proved rich in information. The tavern keeper, Robert Deakin, shared his theories on the lost chest and the broader political landscape, highlighting how many groups were searching for it.

During our time there, we encountered two notable individuals:

* **Rithelm Drake, a human soldier**, who presented as a massive man of average height with a square face and rough, pink skin. He initially demanded a drink but, upon realizing we were adventurers, softened his stance and bought us one. He confessed that he could have been an adventurer himself, but family life had taken precedence. When questioned about the monastery, he was initially confused but eventually recalled **an abandoned monastery near Gallow's Corner**. This was a direct hit on our primary objective.  
* **Gonnathrad Kevanarial, a High Elf Diplomat**, entered the scene, a skinny man with long, wavy black hair and grey eyes, dressed in a fancy red tunic. It quickly became apparent that **Gonnathrad was one of those individuals who had consumed far too many adventure stories from taverns and now desperately wanted to be a part of one himself**. He became overly excited at the prospect of being a "quest giver", even offering to write a five-page backstory for us, replete with intricate details for our adventure. He almost divulged information about the monastery before recalling his actual duty to find adventurers to hunt bandits.

We recognized an opportunity here. Despite his eagerness, Gonnathrad was a diplomat, and his official endorsement could be useful. We obliquely hinted at our knowledge of the monastery and the chest, carefully withholding specifics, which only spurred his enthusiasm. He pressed us for more, but we remained firm. Seizing the moment, he **issued us a letter of permit, officially hiring us to pursue a lead regarding the chest near a monastery by Gallow's Corner**. This letter, he assured us, would secure us **100 gold pieces** upon presentation to Caer Ostverk along with the information. It was clear he was still confused about why we were so focused on an abandoned monastery, but his romanticized view of adventure overshadowed his professional curiosity. As always, gathering information and manipulating circumstances to our advantage was proving more effective than direct confrontation.

### **Zephyr's Perspective**

After collecting the honey reward in Sheernobb and enduring the religious zeal of Calmer in Hommlet, where Zinnia gave up a perfectly good magical trident, our current objective was clear: follow the trail to the lost chest. The notes we found with the Black Earth cultists explicitly mentioned a monastery in the mountains as its location. With that, we set off for Ostverk.

The journey there was quiet, which was fine by me; less trouble, less attention drawn to my, well, *unique* appearance. We found ourselves in "The Cheap Forester" tavern in Ostverk, where a gnome named Robert Deakin owned the place. He was cagey at first, demanding we buy food before talking. I didn't mind; a good meal is always welcome. We picked up on the local rumors about the chest, which seemed to confirm everyone was looking for it. Deakin, though, had his own theories, arguing that the talk of "big evil powers" was just a cover for bandits, and he knew it because he'd fought in the Battle of Emiry Meadows.

While at the tavern, we met a few interesting characters:

* **Rithelm Drake**, a human soldier, was a burly man with a square face and green eyes. He was demanding a drink when we first saw him. When we started asking about the monastery, he seemed confused, but then, surprisingly, he remembered **an abandoned monastery near Gallow's Corner**. Once he realized we were adventurers, he offered to buy us a drink, lamenting that he'd once considered being an adventurer himself before settling down with a wife and three kids. I just nodded; people always had a story, and usually, it involved them not doing what they really wanted.  
* Then there was **Gonnathrad Kevanarial**, a skinny High Elf Diplomat dressed in a fancy red tunic. This guy was a real piece of work. He got ridiculously excited when he thought he could be a "quest giver". He seemed to have read too many adventure stories, wanting to be part of our "narrative" and even offering to write a five-page backstory for us. We gave him just enough information to keep him hooked without revealing too much, and in his eagerness, **he actually gave us a letter of permit**. This letter confirmed he'd hired us to pursue the chest near the monastery at Gallow's Corner, and it promised **100 gold pieces** as a reward when presented to Caer Ostverk. He was so caught up in the "adventure story" of it all, he didn't even seem to care *why* we wanted to go to an abandoned monastery.

Once we had that letter and the confirmed location, we made our exit. I, for one, was eager to **leave Ostverk as quickly as possible**. In a town full of people with agendas, the less attention drawn to us, the better, especially with my appearance. We had the lead, and it was time to follow it.

# Chapter 10.5 Gallows Corner

Here is the detailed breakdown of the session's events:

* **The Interrogation:** Following an ambush where they saved blackmailed innkeepers, Zephyr and Ziinnia employed a "good cop, bad cop" strategy on a captured cultist. Zephyr's highly successful intimidation revealed that Ranus Duval (the "Duke") worked for a larger competing organization, and that the missing chest meant for Romag was likely at an abandoned monastery near Gallows Corner.  
* **Gallows Corner Rumors:** The party traveled to the rural settlement of Gallows Corner to discreetly gather intelligence. Ziinnia successfully eavesdropped on hushed conversations, uncovering suspicious activities involving a merchant named Tilly Bizpebble Whistle, from whom Nicholas purchased a painted rock.  
* **The Earth Cult's Shadow:** Hearing from locals that the unusual shadows and tremors in the hills were tied to Earth cult activity, the party decided to bypass the mining tunnels and take the public path straight to the monastery.  
* **Infiltrating the Old Faith:** At the monastery—which appeared partially abandoned but was dedicated to the nature goddess Beory (referred to as "Barry")—Calmer attempted to deceive an elderly monk by pretending to be lost in the woods.  
* **The Path of Righteousness:** The party met Sister Alina and Brother Eldren, who led them through a series of moral and physical dilemmas known as the "Path of Righteousness" to test their decision-making and worth.  
* **The Trial of Combat:** The session concluded with Sister Alina offering a trial of combat and cunning to prove the party's strength and loyalty, pitting them against a feral gnoll of demonic origin

# Chapter 10.7 The mysteries of the temple

* **The Combat Trial:** The session opened with the party engaging in a grueling, non-lethal combat trial against the monastery's martial monks to test their strength and weaknesses. During the fierce exchange of longswords and short swords, Nicholas was knocked unconscious but was swiftly revived by Calmer's healing magic.  
* **The Interrogation:** Following the bout, Zephyr and Ziinnia ruthlessly interrogated a captive monk, with Zephyr successfully using intimidation to shatter the monastery's peaceful facade. The captive confessed that the monastery was not devoted to the nature goddess Beory, but to the Earth Cult.  
* **The Titan's Awakening:** The captive further revealed that a catastrophic ritual to summon a Titan was already underway, orchestrated by the High Templar Tharok Stevan (Tharok Stonevein).  
* **The Infiltration Plan:** Learning they needed to access the restricted library to stop the ritual, the party forged a tactical alliance with the defector Brother Eldren. They split their forces: Ziinnia and the GM's character would confront the supposed cult leader Sister Alina, with Eldren providing a distraction, while Zephyr and Nicholas infiltrated the library.  
* **The Labyrinthine Library:** Zephyr and Nicholas navigated a magical library filled with floating books, glowing runes, and earth magic. After struggling with initial investigation checks, they deciphered a clue regarding four stones and four passages, ultimately revealing a hidden door by knocking three times on a specific bookshelf.  
* **The Book Swarm:** Beyond the hidden door, they encountered a puzzle requiring four specific books. Searching the shelves for the missing fourth tome triggered the library's magical defenses, surrounding them in a violent swarm of animated books. Zephyr and Nicholas shredded the swarms in combat, causing the remaining books to vanish and revealing the path forward.  
* **The Elemental Riddle:** The session concluded with the party deciphering a cryptic poem about the four elements, correctly deducing that the overarching answer to the riddle was the Temple of Elemental Evil itself

# Chapter 11 Tharok's Fury Unleashed

## **Summary**
