The session began with the dungeon master providing crucial context about the political landscape and ongoing conflicts. The party learned about the tensions between Verbabonc and the gnomes of the Kron Hills, where territorial disputes over valuable gem mines had created a powder keg of conflict. Verbabonc’s construction of a strategic castle in Hommlet served as force projection, much like a medieval aircraft carrier, allowing them to control the valley roads and threaten gnomish territory. The gnomes viewed this expansion as imperialism, while Verbabonc claimed it was necessary protection against the Temple of Elemental Evil.

The party discovered that the various elemental temple factions operated like competing mob families, each controlling different territories and fighting for dominance. Lareth controlled the fire temple territory and had been using the party to eliminate earth temple gangs, expanding his own power base. However, all these factions ultimately answered to the Temple of Elemental Evil, the overarching criminal organization pulling the strings. The Chest of Rao served as the prize everyone sought \- a powerful magical battery capable of fueling devastating rituals according to the laws of magical thermodynamics.

Continuing their exploration of the abandoned monastery, the party encountered Brother Eldrin’s stone guardian in an ancient library. The guardian presented them with a riddle about the eternal cycles of nature, testing their understanding of Beory’s old faith. Zephyr successfully identified the answer as the water cycle, demonstrating wisdom that honored the old ways. The guardian declared their worthiness and became inanimate, opening the passage deeper into the monastery complex.

Moving stealthily through the stone corridors, the party overheard cultists excitedly discussing Tharok’s impending ritual and wishing they could witness the momentous event. Sequioa and Zephyr eliminated the guards silently, finding a key among their possessions. The cultists wore brown robes marked with triangle symbols, indicating their allegiance to the earth temple faction.

The key unlocked a prison chamber where two captives were being held for torture. Jerry, a soldier of Verbabonc, and Warda, a gnome nationalist, were engaged in heated political arguments even while imprisoned. Despite their ideological differences \- Jerry representing Verbabonc’s interests and Warda opposing what she saw as imperial expansion \- both agreed to help fight the cultists after the party explained the dire threat. Jerry revealed crucial information about a secret passageway to the deeper temple, knowledge passed down from his grandmother who had once worshipped Beory in these halls.

Armed with scimitars taken from the fallen cultists, the group followed the secret passage to the heart of the monastery. They emerged into a vast ritual chamber that took their breath away \- ancient stone carved with intricate elemental motifs, towering pillars like colossal trees, and a massive cracked altar at the center. The Chest of Rao rested upon the altar, radiating both protective magic and creeping corruption, while arcane symbols floated above it in eerie greenish-gold light.

High Cultist Tharok stood before the altar, his imposing form marked by skin mottled like cracked stone and eyes burning with fervent intensity. Three robed cultists knelt around him in reverence, their whispered invocations weaving into the elemental hum that filled the air. The floor bore concentric rings of golden runes representing elemental power, and the very air vibrated with raw magical energy that made the chamber feel alive.

When Tharok spotted the intruders, he declared them defilers of his sacred ritual and threatened that the earth itself would swallow them whole. Battle erupted as the party moved to stop the ritual before it could be completed. Zinnia, Sequioa, and Zephyr quickly dispatched the three cultist guards with swift, decisive strikes, while Calmer blessed the party with divine magic to aid their efforts.

Jerry charged bravely toward the high cultist but was struck down by Tharok’s devastating attacks, his body crumpling under the elemental fury. Tharok’s earth magic caused the entire chamber to quake violently, dust and stones cascading from the ceiling as tremors knocked several party members to the ground. The earthquake disrupted Calmer’s blessing as he struggled to maintain his footing on the shifting stone.

Sequioa recognized that disrupting the ritual itself might weaken their enemy and rushed to the altar, knocking the Chest of Rao from its resting place. The magical energies swirling through the chamber immediately began to dissipate as the ritual circle was broken. Zephyr struck with deadly precision, landing a critical blow against the high cultist, while Warda continued fighting despite witnessing Jerry’s death, her hatred for imperial forces now focused on the cultist threat.

The battle raged on as Calmer called upon divine power and Zinnia pressed the attack with relentless strikes. Tharok retaliated with earth-shaking force, hurling Zephyr backward with a devastating blow while his elemental magic continued to make the chamber tremble. Though bloodied and battered, the high cultist remained standing as the session concluded, the outcome of this crucial confrontation still hanging in the balance.

**📝 Session Summary: Triumph and Treachery in Hommlet**  
The session began in a state of high tension and immediate action within the Earth Temple's ritual chamber, located deep beneath the abandoned monastery near Gallow's Corner. High Cultist **Tharok**, his skin mottled like cracked stone, was reeling from having his vital ritual disrupted, but still posed a lethal threat. The gnome nationalist **Warda** opened the round, landing a single strike against the cultist after two initial misses. **Zephyr** followed, landing a strong blow bolstered by a Sneak Attack. The enraged **Tharok** lashed out, striking **Warda** with a devastating blow that dealt 29 damage, though his subsequent attacks missed their mark.

The party pressed the advantage, determined to end the fight before **Tharok** could regain control. **Calmer** targeted the high cultist with a *Guiding Bolt*, dealing 9 damage and granting **Zinnia** advantage on her next strike. **Zinnia** capitalized on the opportunity, launching a flurry of attacks that struck true, dealing a total of 14 points of damage and finally felling the formidable **High Cultist Tharok**.

With **Tharok** dead, the entire cavern began to violently collapse, forcing the party to make a swift exit. After a quick debate, they decided to secure the **Chest of Rao**—the powerful artifact radiating both protective and corruptive energy—and fled through the secret passageway.

### **The Abbey's Reward and the Lore of Rao**

The party arrived safely back in the main abbey, where the damage seemed miraculously contained to the ritual cavern. They found **Brother Eldin**, who was alive but wounded. **Brother Eldin** was relieved to learn that **Tharok** had been vanquished and declared the Abbey of Beory safe once more.

He offered the adventurers a reward for their service:

* **Wealth:** 70 gold pieces, 400 silver pieces, and 400 copper pieces each.  
* **Magic Items:** A **Scroll of Web** and a **Potion of Greater Healing**.

Seeing the party carrying the magical relic, **Brother Eldin** delivered crucial lore regarding the **Chest of Rao**. He explained that **Rao** is the patron of peace and harmony, and the chest is an artifact of **great good** built by the high priests of Rao. He warned that the chest was extremely dangerous to possess, as it would attract both those who would claim it for evil—using it as a powerful magical battery—and the forces of good who would reclaim it for the Archclericy of Veluna. He urged them to return it rather than risk the peril it would draw.

### **Setting the Trap in Hommlet**

Despite the warnings, the party chose to use the **Chest of Rao** as powerful leverage in their ongoing conflict with the Temple's agents in **Hommlet**. They planned to eliminate the traders **Rannos** and **Gremag**, who they knew were working for the Temple of Elemental Evil.

The party sent a note to the agents, requesting a meeting behind the carpenter's field. **Rannos**, suspicious and paranoid, refused the meeting spot but invited them to meet at his establishment after dark. The party retreated to the Inn of the Welcome Wench to wait and plan, where **Calmer** purchased an expensive gnomish mead.

Under the cover of night, the party moved to the shop's back area and lured out their first target, **Thorne (Duke)**, the man-at-arms hired by **Rannos**. They instantly launched a surprise attack, quickly overwhelming the mercenary. **Zephyr** landed multiple hits, followed by **Zinnia**. **Sequoia** delivered the final strike, **knocking Thorne unconscious**. Interrogating the mercenary, they confirmed he was a simple hired hand who would rather be left for dead than face the wrath of the cult.

### **The Decisive Confrontation**

Armed with the unconscious **Thorne**, the party entered the inner office to confront **Rannos** and his bodyguard, **Gremag**. **Rannos** immediately dismissed **Thorne** as useless and demanded the **Chest of Rao**. When the party attempted to negotiate for the Temple to leave the region, **Rannos** and **Gremag** mocked them, and **Gremag** immediately initiated combat, attacking **Zinnia**.

The first round was devastating for the party:

* **Zinnia** and **Zephyr** landed severe blows on **Gremag**, wounding him deeply.  
* **Gremag** retaliated with two swift and brutal strikes, first severely injuring **Zinnia** and then **knocking her unconscious** with the second hit.  
* **Sequoia** continued the assault, dealing a total of 25 points of damage.

In the second round, the tide turned decisively. While **Zinnia** lay dying, **Calmer** finally landed a critical blow, hitting **Gremag** and instantly **killing** the Temple operative. With **Gremag** dead and **Zinnia** downed, the party immediately focused their remaining strength on **Rannos**. **Zephyr** moved to secure a position, granting **Sequoia** the advantage he needed. **Sequoia** struck, wounding **Rannos** for a massive 26 points of damage.

The session concluded with the local leadership of the Temple operation in **Hommlet** annihilated: **Gremag** dead and **Rannos** severely wounded, leaving the **Chest of Rao** in the hands of the victorious adventurers.

# Chapter 12 The Price of Paranoia

The session began in the aftermath of the intense battle in **Hommlet**, with **Gremag** slain and **Zinnia** unconscious, requiring a death save. **Zinnia** successfully stabilized, rolling a 16 on her save, securing her survival.

## **The Double Kill and the Cleanup**

**Zephyr** opened the action, attempting to finish off **Rannos**, who was already severely wounded. While **Zephyr** landed a hit, it was **Calmer** who, for the second time this session, struck the killing blow using his Spiritual Weapon, dispatching **Rannos** (who was down to 2 hit points) with a dramatic flourish. **Calmer** then rushed to **Zinnia** and cast *Cure Light Wounds*, healing her for 24 points.

With both **Rannos** and **Gremag** dead, the party consulted the captured mercenary, **Thorne (the Duke)**, on what to do next. The **Duke** immediately identified **Rannos** and **Gremag** as agents of the **Deep Temple** (the overarching leadership of the Temple of Elemental Evil) and warned of severe retribution. He advised the party on how to frame the scene to avoid suspicion, suggesting they must dismember the bodies and remove the tongues to prevent the use of *Speak with Dead*.

**Thorne** revealed his "cleaning" skills and offered his services to the party on a retainer basis (20 gold pieces per month plus 100 gold pieces per incident). The party agreed to pay him 100 gold pieces for this first cleanup and untied him. **Thorne** confirmed his cynical, money-driven nature and his lack of loyalty to the cult.

## **Treasure and Misdirection**

The party searched the corpses and quarters, finding valuable treasure:

* An **Amulet of Proof against Detection**.  
* A **Ring of Protection \+1**.  
* A **Dagger of Venom**.  
* Three doses of **Dust of Disappearance**.  
* 300 gold pieces and 150 platinum pieces (split among the party).  
* A small black **scarab** inscribed with the glyphs **TZGY**.

**Thorne** executed his cleanup, then instructed **Zinnia** and **Calmer** to take the victims' bloodied clothes and ride out of town to abandon them, simulating a struggle. **Zephyr** and **Sequoia** were told to return to the Inn of the Welcome Wench and spread the story that **Rannos** and **Gremag** had urgently left town after securing a magical chest.

## **Elmo's Revelation and Verbobonc's Play**

The party returned to the Inn of the Welcome Wench. **Calmer**, despite being inwardly shaken by the bloodshed, was reassured by **Zinnia**’s stoic presence. The tavern keeper, **Ostler Goodingoat**, questioned the party about the sudden departure of the merchants, and they successfully bluffed him with the story of the magical chest leaving town.

They were then joined by **Elmo**, a member of the town committee who often acted like a drunken buffoon. **Zinnia**'s high Insight roll (a natural 20\) confirmed the party's suspicions: **Elmo** was feigning inebriation and working for **Verbobonc**. **Zephyr** decided to expose **Elmo**'s subterfuge, and **Elmo** immediately dropped the act, agreeing to speak with the party as equals in a private room.

**Elmo** confirmed he was an agent for **Verbobonc** and was dismayed that the chest had supposedly left with **Rannos** and **Gremag**. He rushed off to contact the rangers to pursue the missing chest. Before leaving, he gave the party an urgent task: return to the **Moathouse** immediately to **"investigate what's going on and destroy whatever evil presence is there"**, concerned that the cultists, now possessing the chest, might enact unknown rituals. He emphasized the political sensitivity—**Verbobonc** could not send troops without risking conflict with the gnomes of the **Kron Hills**.

## **Confrontation with Lareth the Beautiful**

The following morning, **Burne** and **Rufus** approached the party, confirming **Elmo** (now referred to simply as "Elmo") had sent the rangers after the missing chest. They, too, urged the party to deal with the **Moathouse**. The party decided to go directly to **Lareth the Beautiful**'s headquarters to confront him.

They killed two gnoll guards at the secret entrance with ease, and then delivered the dramatic flourish of the severed heads to **Lareth**'s guard. **Lareth** greeted them with predatory suspicion, having heard rumors of the deaths in the Earth Temple. The party attempted to lie, claiming the **Deep Temple** had taken the chest and that **Rannos** and **Gremag** were acting on **Lareth**'s behalf. This maneuver was a strategic attempt to cause infighting within the Temple's ranks.

However, **Lareth** was extremely paranoid. Despite the party succeeding on their group persuasion check, **Lareth** rolled a high check and saw through their deception, furious that they had failed his mission and were now attempting to backstab him. He accused them of treachery. **Zephyr** escalated the situation further by mocking him: "The only person here that's backstabbing is you." **Lareth**'s reaction was immediate and decisive, triggering a call to arms and initiating combat. The session concluded just as the fight with **Lareth** and his guards was about to begin.

# Chapter 13 Decimating the Moathouse and the Call to Nulb

The session resumed with the party positioned in **Lareth the Beautiful**'s inner sanctum in the **Moathouse**, moments after having executed their elaborate misdirection in **Hommlet** following the deaths of **Rannos** and **Gremag**. **Lareth** was furious, having seen through the party's lies that the **Deep Temple** stole the **Chest of Rao**. The room was a tense trap, with **Lareth** and his guards surrounding the adventurers.

## **The Duel with Lareth**

The fight began with **Zephyr** using a dose of **Dust of Disappearance** to become invisible, retreating to a corner to position himself for a later sneak attack. **Zinnia** and **Sequoia** immediately engaged the visible guards, quickly dispatching several of **Lareth**'s goons and carving a path through the room.

**Lareth** retaliated by summoning a **Mirror Image** of himself, confusing his attackers, and severely wounding **Calmer** with *Inflict Wounds*. **Calmer** was forced to burn his healing magic early to stay in the fight. **Zephyr** emerged from invisibility and attacked one of the illusory **Lareth**s, confirming it was a fake before retreating to hide again.

The combat continued in brutal close quarters. **Sequoia** and **Zinnia** traded blows with **Lareth** and the remaining guards, while **Calmer** focused on healing himself under the relentless assault of **Lareth**'s sergeant, Sarge. **Lareth** used his magic to paralyze both **Zephyr** and **Zinnia** with *Hold Person*, immobilizing the two primary damage dealers. However, **Lareth** was down to a single hit point. Seeing his opportunity, **Sequoia**, who was not paralyzed, struck the killing blow, felling **Lareth the Beautiful** and ending the reign of the Fire Temple leader in the **Moathouse**.

## **Loot and Level Up**

With **Lareth** dead, the remaining guards and Sarge were quickly dispatched. The party had successfully cleared out the Moathouse operation. They immediately looted **Lareth**'s body and quarters, finding substantial treasure:

* **Magic Items:** A **Mace of Smiting**, magical Plate Mail (which no one could wear), and a **Ring of Free Action**.  
* **Wealth:** A silver service set worth 2,000 GP, a fire opal necklace worth 3,500 GP, and various gems (ungets) worth 800 GP. This haul resulted in each party member gaining a significant sum of gold.

Critically, following this major victory, all four adventurers **gained a level**, increasing their power and acquiring new abilities, such as **Sequoia**'s Uncanny Dodge.

## **Clearing the Moathouse and the Call to Nulb**

The party contacted **Thorne (Duke)**, agreeing to pay him a 50 GP fee for the clean-up job. To ensure the area was safe for **Thorne** to work, they explored the remaining Moathouse dungeon.

They encountered a large group of eight **Gnolls** who were allies of **Lareth**'s via a local Gnoll King. Using deception, they successfully convinced the **Gnolls** that **Lareth** was merely "busy" and that a pay raise was imminent, stalling them from discovering **Lareth**'s body. They also discovered a secret mechanism that dropped an iron grate, blocking a passage, and found evidence that pulling a seemingly false wall triggered a trap related to the grate.

Further exploration led them to a chamber guarded by six intelligent **Bugbears**. The party used their greatest weapon—chaos—by telling the **Bugbears** that **Lareth** was dead and that the entire **Moathouse** was now ripe for pillaging. They successfully persuaded the **Bugbears** to run off and fight the **Gnolls** for the plunder.

Finally, the party returned to **Hommlet** and gathered the local council members: **Osler Goodingut**, **Burne**, **Rufus**, and **Jaroo**. They presented their undeniable proof of the Temple of Elemental Evil's resurgence and the death of **Lareth**. **Jaroo** asserted that the next step was vital: the party must travel to the city of **Nulb** to infiltrate the Temple's operations there, disrupt their machinations, and identify who was truly in charge. The council members agreed that while they prepared the militia, the adventurers needed to continue their disruptive work in secret. The session concluded with the adventurers poised to head north to **Nulb**, still in possession of the invaluable **Chest of Rao**.

# Chapter 14 From Brothel to Bonfire

## **Summary**

The party arrived in the village of Nulb, a place notorious for its loose morals and unsavory reputation. After meeting with local guards at the Drowned Cottage, who warned them about the town’s dangerous nature, they explained they were seeking work for those willing to commit crimes and misdemeanors. As they explored the settlement, passing landmarks like the boatwright, Waterstead Hostel, Nulb Stables, and Gallows Square, they noticed signposts from Constable Eldrin Malavar warning citizens about a condemned temple south of town and declaring the Pilgrim’s Trail off-limits to all.

At the Boatman’s Tavern, they met Skole, the owner and bartender, along with Ketta, a serving wench. Their investigation led them to the Rhennee encampment, where they encountered Eryk Velkori, the caravan leader, who was consumed by grief over his missing sister, Serena. The usually vibrant camp was unnaturally still, the air thick with ash and river rot, as Eryk clutched a bottle of rum and wept openly. He explained that Serena had vanished after a night at the Boatman’s Tavern, and he believed she had been taken by the Zelrads, the Reapers, or Nisera’s Talons through a place called the Iron Lash. When Sequioa jokingly asked about a map to hell, Eryk responded that he was already living in one, his tragedy serving as the very path to damnation.

Eryk provided crucial information about Nulb’s criminal underworld. He revealed that the Crimson Reapers, led by a man named Zelt the Rat, sought to dominate the town’s criminal activities and funnel supplies through the area. More ominously, he spoke of Madame Selentis, also known as Nisera, who was the true power in Nulb—a member of a disgraced noble family from Verbobonc who now controlled everything like a spider in a web. Eryk warned that if Madame Selentis ever took notice of them, things would have gone very badly indeed. When asked about the Iron Lash, he laughed heartily and revealed it was simply the name of the local brothel.

Eryk’s daughter Tala then approached the party, inviting them to meet with her mother, Vethra Velkori, for a divination. The elderly Rhennee woman sat cross-legged in the shadows of her wagon, surrounded by bones, blackened cards, and a copper bowl that smelled of clove and blood. Her clouded blue eyes seemed to look through them rather than at them as she offered to read their fates. The party chose smoke for the first reading, and Vethra burned a lock of braided black hair, revealing visions of a crooked house with shifting shadows, a tavern with too many secrets called the Waterside, and a man with a red sash who dealt in whispers and iron.

For the second reading, the party chose bone, and Vethra tossed wolf knuckles and pigeon vertebrae into a shallow bowl. The bones clicked and scattered, forming a pattern that made her voice darken as she declared that Serena might be on her knees but was not broken, not with Rhennee blood in her veins. Despite her son Eryk’s protests that the divination would make her sick or even kill her, Vethra insisted on drawing cards from her faded Tarokka deck. She revealed The Chain, The Betrayer, and The Flooded Gate, then drew a fourth card with a hiss of breath—a crimson-painted barge sailing low on the Velverdyva, heavy with souls that no longer sang. Serena had been taken there, bound and traded like meat. Exhausted, Vethra collapsed back, and the party felt the weight of occult promises settling upon them as Eryk declared they had indeed found their map to hell.

The party returned to the Boatman’s Tavern in the late evening, where they observed various groups including a gentleman with guards, a ship captain with river pirates, and an old woman who feigned disinterest while carefully watching everything. They approached the serving wenches and spoke with Ketta, who nervously revealed there had been a fight involving Serena and the Rhennee, but she pointed fearfully toward the pirate captain, clearly too terrified to say more. The party then approached Captain Tolub, a boisterous and intimidating figure who made an awful joke that his crew laughed at with exaggerated enthusiasm. After some negotiation and charm from the party, Tolub agreed to buy them drinks and asked about their business in Nulb, questioning why they would help a man like Eryk Velkori.

When the party explained they might look like bandits but still had heart, Tolub laughed heartily at their sentimentality. He revealed that Serena had been taken to the Iron Lash as payment for something, and when the party asked where the brothel was located, he mockingly informed them it was upstairs in the very same building. The party ascended the stairs to find a low-ceiling, smoky lounge guarded by a massive man with an iron cudgel. They demanded to speak with the boss and were directed to Elvanna’s chambers, where they met a gorgeous woman with orange-red hair who spoke with a posh accent. Elvanna confirmed she held Serena as compensation for damages caused by the Rhennee at the Boatman’s Tavern and demanded five hundred gold pieces for her release.

The party paid the sum without negotiation, which surprised Elvanna, who remarked that she might have asked for more if she had known they were that desperate. Serena was brought from the pits, appearing dazed but defiant, and as she left, Elvanna mockingly wished to see her again, to which Serena retorted with a wish for Elvanna to go to hell. The party escorted Serena back to the Rhennee camp as quickly as possible, where she explained she had been used as leverage for a debt she didn’t know about and that her father was being coerced into ambushes along the forest road. Upon their arrival, Eryk’s eyes lit up with joy, and the entire camp erupted in music and dancing, the previous gloom completely dispelled.

Eryk declared the party family and invited them to spend the night celebrating with them. After a long night of drinking and dancing, they woke to find all their belongings intact, confirming Eryk’s honesty and his formal welcome of them as members of the Rhennee clan. He offered them protection and friendship in Nulb, warning that in this town, friends were worth a lot, but they shouldn’t trust all their friends. The party also discovered they had been credited with five hundred gold pieces for Serena’s rescue, the same amount they had paid for her release.

Seeking to advance their mission, the party returned to find Captain Tolub at his boat. When they expressed their desire to contact the higher powers or prophets of the area, specifically mentioning Romag, Tolub directed them to the Hall of the Scarlet Moon, a small encampment a day’s hike northeast of Nulb in the Kron Hills. Before departing, the party consulted Eryk Velkori, who described the Hall as a place where a druid named Elazar taught the rite of the wicker giant to restore nature’s balance. Eryk mentioned that several groups had camped there in festive anticipation, though he noted that many people seemed to be burning fire all day, which struck him as unusual.

The party traveled south through Hommlet, stopping at the tavern where Ostler Goodingoot, the owner and council member, directed them to speak with Jaroo, a local druid. Jaroo expressed deep suspicion about the Circle of the Scarlet Moon, finding them too showy, flashy, and ecstatic for true druids, suggesting their practices were more religious than natural. Despite these warnings, the party continued past Shernobb into the Kron Hills, where they arrived at the Hall of the Scarlet Moon. They observed a square tower ringed by ruined walls, a massive bonfire shaped like a fiery giant, and seven smaller bonfires with tents clustered around them.

The party attempted to approach stealthily and first visited Camp Number One, where they met four friendly human druids named Verigo, Mahoon, Iniri, and Faria. These druids were excited about the upcoming rite of the wicker giant and offered the party food, drink, and brandy, inviting them to stay the night. Deciding to scout further, the party moved to Camp Number Two, where they encountered two robed men named Albrith and Ingulf, who identified themselves as circuits of the Scarlet Moon. When the party asked whether they were related to the Temple of Elemental, the robed figures adopted threatening poses and revealed their allegiance by stating “fire, water, earth, or air,” confirming their connection to elemental evil.

The party immediately realized they had been set up by Captain Tolub, who had sent them to the Fire Temple instead of the Earth Temple. As they attempted to flee, the fire priests unleashed devastating magical attacks, hurling explosive bursts of flame that severely wounded the party and knocked Calmer unconscious. Zephyr quickly used a magical dust to render the entire party invisible, and Sequioa grabbed the fallen Calmer while the others scattered in different directions. To create a diversion, Zephyr cut the rope holding one of the leashed bears, unleashing the enraged animal upon the priests. Unable to see their invisible quarry and distracted by the rampaging bear, the fire clerics could only watch in fury as the party escaped into the hazy wilderness, having learned a harsh lesson about the dangers of direct confrontation and the value of stealth and cunning in their quest.

# Chapter 15 From Fire to Forest: Tolubs treachery

## **Summary**

The party found themselves catching their breath in the wilderness after a harrowing escape from the Hall of the Scarlet Moon. They had been betrayed by Captain Tolub, who sent them into a trap at the fire temple instead of connecting them with Romag of the Earth Temple as promised. Fire priests Albreth and Ingulf had unleashed devastating magical flames upon them, nearly killing Calmer before Zephyr’s quick thinking with magical dust allowed them to vanish and flee into the darkness. Now, hidden in the hills with their hearts still racing, they debated their next move.

Calmer, recovered from his brush with death, insisted they needed to change their approach. He declared he was done being the face of the party, tired of looking like a fool and nearly getting killed. The party’s strength, he argued, lay in infiltration and stealth, not direct confrontation. After some discussion, they agreed on a plan: return to Nulb and confront Tolub, proving they had survived whatever test he had thrown at them, and demand an audience with the temple leadership. Zinnia’s concern for Calmer’s wellbeing—though he claimed it was merely because reviving him was annoying—brought a moment of levity to their tense situation.

The party chose to travel stealthily through the forest rather than risk being seen in towns. Zinnia’s expertise proved invaluable as he found them a well-hidden cave to rest in and carefully obscured their tracks. During the night, patrols passed nearby but failed to discover their hiding spot. As they continued their journey the following day, they encountered a suspicious patch of soft ground that collapsed when poked, revealing a nest of angry poisonous snakes that they carefully avoided.

Later, while moving quietly through the woods, they spotted a group of gnolls dressed in fire temple garb. The party successfully hid as the creatures marched past, clearly searching for something or someone. Sequioa noticed the gnolls seemed to be on patrol, possibly looking for the party themselves or dealing with the various bandit factions that plagued the region. The encounter reinforced how dangerous the area had become and how carefully they needed to move.

As they drew closer to Nulb, the party observed a bandit chief leading another group of gnolls through the forest. Sequioa’s keen hearing picked up the chief muttering complaints about recruiting gnolls from the local gnoll king for the fire temple. This revelation was troubling—the Temple of Elemental Evil wasn’t just surviving, it was actively growing and recruiting forces. The party realized that stopping individual gnoll groups would be futile; they needed to eliminate whoever was doing the recruiting. They also learned more about the temple’s structure: four cults led by prophets—Romag of Earth, Belsornig of Water, and two unnamed leaders of Fire and Air—all ruled by a mysterious deep temple.

Upon reaching Nulb, the party made their way to the Boatman’s Tavern. Skole, the bartender, was visibly shocked to see them alive. He revealed that Tolub had been telling everyone he had disposed of the party, claiming they were meddlers who didn’t belong. Taking them into his back room, Skole confided that he despised both Tolub and Lodriss, the tavern’s actual owner, and wished to be free of them both. More importantly, he told them that if they wanted to contact the Earth Temple, they needed to speak with Dick Rentsch at the Waterside Hostel, not Tolub, who worked for the fire prophet.

The party walked through Nulb toward the Waterside Hostel, passing Gallows Table where they saw a dead man hanging from the gallows—Elfar Twell, executed on the orders of the powerful Madame Selentis for the crime of crossing her. They encountered various merchant stalls, including Magda’s Trove with its strange magical curiosities like cursed ink, and Garik’s armory selling rusted weapons. These encounters painted a picture of Nulb as a desperate, dangerous place where power was everything.

At the Waterside Hostel, a dilapidated building with sagging timbers and lopsided walls, they met Dick Rentsch. The gruff proprietor sized them up with suspicion and warned them he ran a tight operation that didn’t tolerate trouble. When the party mentioned they had something of interest for the Earth Temple, Dick Rentsch’s demeanor shifted to excitement. He took them to his back office for a private conversation, though they noticed the bandit chief from the forest was also present in the tavern, suggesting this was neutral ground where various factions coexisted uneasily.

While waiting for Dick Rentsch to return that evening, the party was approached by Wat, the bartender, who attempted to extract information about their business. The party deflected his questions, claiming it was a personal matter. Wat seemed relieved and mentioned someone named Dala before warning them not to drink the beer and serving them gin instead. When Dick Rentsch finally returned, he explained that to earn his trust and the prophet’s favor, they needed to complete a mission: ambush and kill a group of gnolls and their handlers who were being recruited for the fire temple that very night.

The party returned to the forest and positioned themselves for the ambush. When the bandit chief appeared with his gnoll recruits, he seemed paranoid, muttering that Dick Rentsch might be up to something. The party struck swiftly and decisively. Sequioa and Zinnia focused their attacks on the bandit chief, cutting him down before he could react. Calmer created a terrifying booming voice that froze the gnolls in fear, while Zephyr methodically eliminated them one by one.

The battle was brutal but efficient. Sequioa delivered a devastating strike that literally cut one gnoll in half, while Zinnia moved with deadly precision through the chaos. The remaining gnolls, driven by bloodlust despite their fear, fought desperately but were overwhelmed. The last bandit, realizing his leader was dead and contemplating his poor life choices, made a futile attack before being struck down. When the dust settled, the party stood victorious over the bodies of their enemies, having proven themselves capable of the ruthless work the Earth Temple required. They had successfully completed Dick Rentsch’s test and earned their entry into the Temple of Elemental Evil.

# Chapter 16 A Corridor Paved with Bones

## **Summary**

The party stood over their captured gnoll, bound and snarling at their feet. Calmer, the cleric of St. Cuthbert, burned with righteous fury at the sight of the creature—a spawn of demon powers born by bursting from the bodies of hyenas. When Zephyr attempted to politely question the prisoner, Calmer suggested a more forceful approach. Zephyr slapped the gnoll and demanded information, but the creature only hissed and threatened to rip them to shreds. With the interrogation clearly failing, Calmer swiftly ended the gnoll’s life, his hatred for these demonic beings overriding any further attempts at diplomacy.

Searching the bodies, the party discovered several items of interest: a scroll bearing ancient markings, three ornate gold buttons, and symbols marking the dead as servants of the Temple of Fire. With their grim task complete, they made their way back to Nulb and the Waterside Hostel, where their contact Dick Rentsch awaited them. In the back room, Dick Rentsch confirmed that he had arranged a meeting with Romag, a powerful figure within the temple they sought. Calmer, ever cautious and weary of being killed yet again, insisted on taking precautions while they waited. He moved a heavy chest to barricade the door and positioned Zephyr to watch the window, muttering about his growing exhaustion with making deals with evil powers that wanted them all dead.

When Dick Rentsch returned, he led them out of Nulb along a rutted, weed-choked road toward their destination. The path was spotted with rank vegetation—thistles, burrs, thorns, and nettles—and though it appeared little-used, one of the party members noticed signs that many creatures of various sizes had recently traveled this way, with someone working hard to disguise the heavy traffic. As they approached, the landscape grew increasingly disturbing. Dead trees stood like skeletons, and twisted, unnaturally colored vegetation surrounded them. Skulls and bones gleamed white among the weeds, some covered in strange fungus none of them had ever seen before.

The Temple of Elemental Evil loomed before them, a massive structure far larger than they had imagined. A ruined tower jutted from the northeast corner of shattered walls, and the temple itself stood like a leprous gray monument to darkness. Its arched buttresses were obscene, covered in climbing vegetation, and every surface was carved with leering faces and writhing forms depicting unspeakable obscenities. A pervading sense of fear hung over the entire area like a tangible cloud of vileness. Sounds seemed distorted, and movements flickered at the edges of their vision. The wind moaned through hundreds of apertures in the temple walls, creating sounds like the wails of doomed souls, while ravens croaked and flapped among the ruins.

The main entrance featured two pairs of massive bronze doors, twenty-three feet high and twenty feet wide, held fast by huge iron chains with all cracks sealed. Graven upon these doors were glowing runes that burned with silvery radiance, making their eyes water to look upon. Dick Rentsch carefully avoided these runes, leading them instead to a side entrance. The party recalled a story from Spugnoir in Hommlet about the Circle of Eight—powerful wizards including the legendary Mordenkainen and Bigby—who had come here and performed mighty magic to seal this place.

Dick Rentsch pulled them inside through the side door into a vestibule. The floor was covered in reddish-brown slate-like tiles, and the plastered walls were painted with horrifying scenes of killing, torture, enslavement, robbery, and unspeakable acts. Dim light filtered through stained glass windows, casting revolting colors across the floor. Pinkish mineral pillars shot through with warm-colored veins supported arches decorated with vile reliefs. Dick Rentsch led them deeper into the temple, to a chamber containing a central altar of pinkish-white marble carved with a disturbing human-shaped depression stained dark with old blood. North of the altar yawned a circular pit twenty feet in diameter, surrounded by shards of broken crystal vessels.

Dick Rentsch seemed disturbingly at ease here, describing it as his happy place. When asked why the temple was so empty, he laughed and explained that once the Earth Temple destroyed the other false prophets, this place would be filled with supplicants who would kill and slaughter and bury alive all who opposed them. He then led them down into the pit itself, where a narrow ledge spiraled down the northern face to steep steps below. As the party prepared to descend, Calmer asked if they could use rope for safety. Dick Rentsch sighed in exasperation, muttering that he thought they were getting better recruits this time, but allowed them to tie themselves together before beginning the long descent.

At the bottom, Dick Rentsch knocked on a wall in a particular pattern, and a secret door opened to reveal a corridor at least one hundred feet long and twenty feet wide. The entire passage was covered in bones—a terrible jumble of human and humanoid skeletons, many still wearing sundered armor or clutching broken weapons, testifying to a great battle that had taken place here. Dick Rentsch warned them to be careful not to disturb the dead as he picked his way through the grim remains. As they followed him eastward, several gnoll skeletons suddenly animated and rose from the bone pile to attack.

The party fought desperately against the undead creatures. Sequioa struck down the first skeleton with a powerful blow, while the remaining skeletons attacked, wounding both Sequioa and Zephyr. Zephyr retreated and struck back, while Zinnia delivered a devastating blow to another skeleton. Calmer raised his holy symbol and called upon the power of St. Cuthbert, destroying one skeleton outright and damaging others with divine radiance. The party quickly finished off the remaining undead, and Dick Rentsch urged them to move quickly before more skeletons could animate. They ran through the rest of the corridor, emerging into a guarded area.

Four guards stationed in a torch-lit hall questioned Dick Rentsch, who explained that the party were the new recruits he had promised to Romag. The guards expressed their loyalty to the Earth Temple and their hatred for the Fire Temple, boasting that once they killed enough gnolls, Alremm—the head of the Fire Temple—would be theirs. The party passed through a barracks area and a lounge decorated with scenes of drinking and debauchery, finally arriving at a well-furnished chamber where two commanders stood guard. Dick Rentsch asked if he needed to confiscate their weapons or if they were smart enough not to try anything stupid. The party assured him of their cooperation, though there was some lighthearted banter about cutting off Zephyr’s tiefling horns.

They were brought before Romag, the High Priest of the Earth Temple, who sat in his lavishly decorated chamber. Romag greeted them warmly, pleased that Dick Rentsch had finally brought him something worthy of his time. He asked for the Chest of Rao, and the party presented it to him. When the party seemed uncertain about the temple’s scope, Romag laughed heartily at their ignorance. He explained that the surface temple was merely the beginning, and that the true power lay in the deeper levels descending into the very pit of evil itself. The first two levels were divided among the Earth, Fire, Water, and Air temples, all locked in constant conflict. Below that lay the deeper temple and deeper secrets. Romag’s mission for them was clear: destroy the other temples so the Earth Temple could seize ultimate power. Dick Rentsch provided them with a map showing where the gnolls could be found, explaining that they would need to navigate through territory controlled by multiple hostile factions. Romag promised that once they proved themselves capable of operating within the temple by completing this mission, they would earn the right to wear the badge of Earth.

The party descended once more, running quickly past the skeleton-filled corridor to avoid another encounter. They arrived in a large octagonal room fifteen feet below the normal dungeon floor, illuminated by an eerie milky radiance emanating from the ceiling, walls, and floor. The polished gray stone floor, shot through with glittering mica, was partially obscured by swirling, glowing mist. In the center stood a great pierced bronze square, and a circular shaft opened above a pit of the same diameter. South of the pit sat an alabaster block bearing two knives and a crystal bowl, while crystal braziers suspended from silver chains emitted sickly sweet perfume smoke. Bronze doors to the north were sealed with iron chains and warning runes identical to those on the main entrance above.

The party dropped into the pit, and immediately a wraith materialized before them—a terrifying undead spirit of malevolent power. Zephyr struck first, landing blows with her dagger, but the wraith retaliated with devastating force. Its spectral touch drained Zephyr’s very life essence, not only wounding him but reducing his maximum vitality in a way that could only be restored through extended rest or powerful healing magic. Calmer called upon St. Cuthbert’s divine power once more, causing the wraith to recoil in fear and suffer holy damage. As the frightened wraith retreated, Calmer moved away, stating grimly that he was beginning to think Dick hadn’t told them everything about what awaited them here.

The party regrouped, realizing with growing concern that they were standing in what was clearly the Air Temple’s power source. Dick Rentsch had not simply sent them down a hallway—he had deposited them directly into enemy territory belonging to a rival elemental faction. Whether this was a test of their abilities or something more sinister remained unclear, but one thing was certain: they were now deep within the Temple of Elemental Evil, surrounded by ancient evil and warring factions, with only their wits and weapons to see them through the trials ahead.

# Chapter 17 Romag’s Tests and Krell’sTreachery

## **Summary**

The party stood in the eerie octagonal room deep beneath the Temple of Elemental Evil, having just escaped a terrifying wraith that had drained Zephyr’s very life essence. The chamber glowed with an unnatural milky red light, swirling mist obscuring the polished gray floor shot through with glittering mica. Calmer muttered that Dick Rentsch hadn’t told them everything about what awaited them in these depths, and the party realized with growing concern that they stood in the Air Temple’s power source—deposited directly into enemy territory.

Following the map Dick Rentsch had provided, the party made their way through a processional hallway painted with scenes of gross evil, death, and destruction. Bronze doors lined the southern walls, their polished surfaces carved with vile scenes and runes. The party understood this was where clerics would drag their sacrificial captives during religious ceremonies, leading them toward the various elemental temples that warred for dominance in this cursed place.

When they reached the designated door and knocked, they entered a room that felt entirely different from the rest of the temple. The floor was white marble, the walls and vaulted ceiling sheathed in pearly alabaster, creating a right feel—a place of safety in a storm of evil. The room was bare and clean, and as they looked around, a sheet of cloudy crystal substance set into the northern portion of the east wall began to brighten, its radiance lighting the chamber. Two man-like creatures with wings of snowy feathers and radiant faces appeared amidst glowing, cloud-tinted billows, robed in purest white with a pale blue sky visible between them.

The brighter of the two radiant figures peered at the party and spoke in dulcet tones, asking what fair and just folk were doing in this foul place of pain and base wickedness. When the party explained they were on a mission to find gnolls and defeat the Temple of Elemental Evil, the figures seemed delighted. The brighter figure then made an unusual request: the party should place every weapon, holy symbol, magic item, and silvered item before the crystal window, then step out and count to seventy-seven while the figures sent a ray of just white through the room to bless their equipment, doubling its efficacy for seven hours.

The party hesitated, their instincts warning them something was amiss. Zephyr offered only his short sword, Zinnia gave a single dagger, and Sequioa reluctantly parted with one dagger as well. When the radiant figures pressed them to surrender more weapons, insisting they surely wouldn’t refuse such a divine blessing, the party stood firm in their refusal. Disappointed, the angelic figures declared they would reserve their blessing for someone else and vanished, leaving the party to wonder what trap they had narrowly avoided.

As they exited through the eastern door, Zinnia’s keen eyes spotted a secret entrance. Opening it discreetly, they discovered a bare room permeated by a feral odor, with nesting heaps of cloth and pillows, bones and litter covering the floor, and rust-red cloaks hanging on pegs. Inside were two werewolves who snarled at the intruders, their eyes gleaming with predatory intelligence. One of them laughed darkly, saying this could have been much easier if they had just dropped all their weapons and come in unarmed—they supposed they would have to do it the hard way for the Fire Temple.

The battle was fierce and desperate. The party quickly realized their weapons were ineffective against the lycanthropic creatures, their blades seeming to pass through flesh without causing proper harm. Calmer called upon his divine power, channeling energy to temporarily enchant Sequioa’s short sword and Zinnia’s spear with magical properties. Zephyr’s dagger already possessed such enchantment, and Calmer wielded his mace of smiting. The werewolves fought with savage ferocity, their claws and fangs tearing into the party, but Calmer’s radiant guardians materialized around him, burning the creatures with holy light. Zinnia landed a devastating blow with his enchanted spear, and together the party brought down both werewolves, standing victorious but battered.

Calmer declared there had better be some magical items in the lair after such a difficult fight. Searching through the room, they discovered a silver service worth a considerable sum, which they divided among themselves. More importantly, they found a magical dagger and short sword, which they distributed to those who still lacked enchanted weapons. But it was Zinnia who made the most crucial discovery—a loose flagstone that concealed a hidden treasure pile. Beneath it lay silver pieces, holy symbols of Saint Cuthbert and Rao, and most critically, five silver daggers and nineteen silver-tipped arrows. The irony was not lost on them: the very equipment they had needed to fight the werewolves had been hidden in the werewolves’ own lair.

The party decided to return to Romag and report what they had found. They carefully navigated back through the dangerous corridors, sneaking past the wraith in the octagonal room and moving silently through the bone-covered hallway where skeletal warriors lay waiting to animate. When they finally reached Romag’s chamber, they found Dick Rentsch sitting beside the high priest. Romag was pleased to see them alive, as Dick Rentsch had apparently wagered they would not survive. When the party explained they had encountered werewolves instead of gnolls, Romag turned sharply to Dick Rentsch, who admitted that Alremm had hired werewolves in the secret chamber as a test of the new recruits.

Romag expressed his approval that the party had not fallen for the angelic trap, recognizing their wisdom and caution. He handed them badges of the Earth Temple, declaring they had proven their worth and would have a place among his followers. That night, the party rested in the bunk rooms with the other guards, where Calmer boldly claimed to be a former cleric of Saint Cuthbert who had converted to the Earth Temple’s cause. His declaration swayed the other guards, who saw the conversion of a holy man as proof of the Earth Temple’s righteousness.

The next morning, Lieutenant Krell appeared with a mission from Romag. The Fire Temple had seized a portion of their level, and the party was to raid them, killing as many as they could without getting themselves killed. He explained that Earth Temple gnolls occupied one area while Fire Temple gnolls held another, and the rivals periodically clashed in brutal combat. The party, having shown more subtlety than the typical gnoll, was tasked with tipping the balance in the Earth Temple’s favor.

The party moved stealthily to the designated location and entered a chamber that had once been a plush guest chamber but was now dirty and cluttered. They surprised the creature, making quick work of the creature. but as they examined its body, a terrible realization dawned on them—the ogre was a member of the Earth Temple, not the Fire Temple. Someone had set them up, and the most likely culprit was Lieutenant Krell himself.

Furious at the betrayal, the party returned to Romag to explain what had happened. When they arrived, Lieutenant Krell was conspicuously absent. Romag listened to their account, and his rage became palpable as he declared he had known the lieutenant was a spy and had kept him under watch. With fury burning in his eyes, Romag commanded the party to bring the traitor to him alive so he could sacrifice him in the Temple of Earth as a warning to all who would dare betray them. The party now faced a new mission: hunting down a cunning spy who had nearly gotten them killed, knowing that the treacherous politics of the Temple of Elemental Evil made trust a dangerous commodity.

# Chapter 18 Welcome to the Temple: Home Sweet Base

## Summary

Romag woke the party after eight hours of rest with urgent news. The traitor had been discovered—Lieutenant Krell had fled to the gnoll and bugbear territories, plotting rebellion against the Earth Temple. Romag outlined a plan to trap the traitor: Earth Temple forces would guard the entrance and exit while the party approached from behind through the dangerous gnoll-infested area. As the party prepared for their mission, they overheard rumors about Romag being a new and inexperienced leader trying to prove himself, known for micromanaging and lacking the cleverness of his predecessor, who had been promoted to the deep temple. The Earth Temple's strategy relied on overwhelming enemies with masses of low-level troops rather than the powerful monsters employed by the Fire, Water, and Air Temples.

The party devised a clever strategy to convince the Earth Temple guards to mount a frontal assault on the gnolls as a distraction. Their persuasion succeeded easily, as the human guards harbored deep fears of being replaced by gnolls and becoming their food. However, when the guards advanced into the gnoll territory, disaster struck. Screams of "It's a trap\!" echoed through the corridors as the gnolls sprang their ambush, catching the guards in nets and slaughtering them mercilessly. Zinnia and Zephyr crept forward to investigate and witnessed a horrifying scene of gnolls feeding on the fallen guards.

The party engaged the remaining gnolls in swift combat, with Calmer casting powerful magic that summoned spectral guardians to devastate the clustered enemies. As they fought, they heard Lieutenant Krell's voice encouraging the gnolls, revealing his betrayal: "I told you they would try to kill you. The Fire Temple protects its own." The party pressed their advantage, cutting through the gnolls with precision strikes. When Krell emerged, wielding a peculiar hand crossbow contraption—a pistol of his own devising—he targeted Calmer directly, firing twice and knocking the cleric unconscious with devastating shots. Sequioa quickly revived Calmer with a healing potion, and the grateful cleric thanked him for his loyalty.

Surrounded and desperate, Krell attempted to negotiate with the party. He argued that the Earth Temple used its followers as mere cannon fodder, while the Fire Temple under Alremm valued power and glory, offering to join forces to destroy the Earth Temple and be hailed as heroes. The party rejected his offer, choosing to remain loyal to Romag. They attacked Krell carefully, using non-lethal strikes to subdue him rather than kill him, as Romag had demanded he be brought back alive. After a brief but intense fight, Sequioa delivered the final blow that knocked Krell unconscious.

The party searched Krell's quarters and the surrounding rooms, discovering considerable treasure including platinum pieces hidden in a brazier, gold, a three-dragon ante set, and useful supplies like flasks of oil. They also found a secret door leading deeper into the complex. With their prisoner secured, they brought the bloodied and unconscious Krell before Romag, who was immensely pleased with their success. He declared that everyone must witness the consequences of betrayal and ordered the party to attend a ritual sacrifice in the great chamber.

The party was led through ornate corridors of polished brown marble veined with black and inlaid with yellow triangular patterns, lit by burning bronze crescents. They entered a vast ritual chamber with rough-hewn sandstone walls and a floor of dark brown earth, dimly illuminated by phosphorescent lichen. At the center stood a twenty-foot pyramid of hard-packed dirt topped with a stone column bearing carved triangles and bronze knives. Romag stood atop the pyramid and began chanting to the elder elemental earth god, shoving dirt into Krell's mouth as the traitor screamed about being tricked. Four massive earth elementals rose from the ground at Romag's command and slowly approached the altar. As the crowd chanted and cheered, the elementals tore Krell's body apart in a gruesome display of the Earth Temple's brutal justice before descending back into the earth.

The party carefully blended in with the cheering crowd, feigning enthusiasm for the horrific spectacle to avoid suspicion. After the ritual concluded, they followed Romag and his lieutenant Hartsch, overhearing them discuss how the defeat had cost valuable resources and weakened their plans to overwhelm the Fire Temple. Hartsch suggested they could use their new allies—the party—as weapons against the Fire or Air Temple. The party noticed Romag disappearing through a secret door and decided to explore the area further.

Their exploration led them to a charred library filled with the burned remains of books on evil philosophy, double-dealing, self-advancement, and treachery. Among the remnants, they found references to the sacred earth triangle and warnings about trials of earthy terrors awaiting the foolhardy in the depths below. They discovered another secret door before continuing their investigation. Eventually, they encountered four guards who challenged them, but the party identified themselves as new Earth Temple recruits. The guards mentioned rumors about Krell's death creating opportunities for promotion, revealing the cutthroat nature of advancement within the temple.

Commander Joradin arrived and questioned the party's presence in his area. Though he acknowledged their status as new recruits, he asserted his authority over that section of the temple and ordered them to return to their quarters. The party, unwilling to accept this restriction, returned to Romag to question why Joradin could deny them access. Romag, still basking in what he considered a great victory despite the heavy losses, explained that Joradin was a new recruit brought in by Dick Rentsch who didn't yet understand how things worked in the temple.

Realizing the party needed proper accommodations, Romag granted them the territory they had cleared of gnolls and bugbears as their own base within the Earth Temple. He provided them with written permission to explore freely and warned them sternly not to betray his trust. He explained that the Earth Temple's territory extended throughout specific areas of the complex, and the party now controlled a significant portion of it. However, he also cautioned that having territory made them targets, as promotion within the temple often came by stepping over the bodies of rivals. The party had successfully infiltrated the Temple of Elemental Evil, earned Romag's trust, and established their own base of operations deep within the enemy stronghold, positioning themselves perfectly to continue their dangerous mission of espionage and sabotage.

# Chapter 19 A Ghoul Time Was Had By All

## Summary

The party awoke in their newly granted quarters within the Earth Temple, the former chambers of the traitor Lieutenant Krell. The room, though marked by blood and the remnants of their recent battles with gnolls, now served as their base of operations. With a secret door and strategic access points, it was a foothold in the heart of enemy territory. The party gathered to discuss their next moves, weighing their options between exploring the dungeon complex, returning to the Fire Temple agents in Nulb to report Krell's death, or consolidating their position. Ultimately, they decided to explore the dungeon to better understand the labyrinthine complex they had infiltrated.

Their exploration began cautiously as they ventured west from their quarters. Opening a door, they encountered two ghasts—undead creatures with hollow eyes and an unsettling hunger. The ghasts questioned whether the party were members of the Earth Temple and, more ominously, whether they had brought food or were food themselves. Nick, playing Sequioa, quickly closed the door on the threatening creatures. Zinnia, observing the interaction, deduced that the Earth Temple forces and the ghasts were not allied, a valuable piece of intelligence about the fractured nature of the temple's inhabitants.

Continuing their exploration northward, the party discovered an abandoned kitchen, a vast chamber twenty feet by thirty feet. A massive fireplace dominated the north wall, large enough to roast an entire ox, with numerous side ovens built into its structure. The room was in ruins, littered with broken containers, dishes, and unidentifiable trash. Skeletons lay scattered across the floor, including the massive bones of an ogre, testament to some past violence. The party investigated the adjacent storage rooms, finding only spoiled food and shattered crockery. As Zephyr approached the fireplace to examine it more closely, a large giant snake suddenly erupted from within, hissing with hunger and fury.

The battle with the snake was swift but intense. Zephyr struck first with his short sword, wounding the creature as it lunged at him with venomous fangs but missed. Zinnia joined the fray, her attacks landing with devastating precision. Calmer invoked dark magic against the serpent, though it initially resisted his spell. Sequioa, despite some initial difficulty, managed to wound the creature further. The snake, desperate and hungry, continued its assault on Sequioa but failed to connect. Calmer's second invocation proved more effective, and finally Zinnia delivered a killing blow that left the snake lifeless on the kitchen floor. Searching the area, the party discovered a short sword and twenty gold pieces among the debris.

Pressing onward, the party entered a vast hall that stretched over a hundred feet in length and thirty feet in width. The chamber told a story of past grandeur and recent violence. Broken tables, trestles, benches, and chairs lay scattered and heaped throughout the space, evidence of great revelries that had once been conducted in this evil place. Tattered tapestries and drapes still clung to the walls, and bones—both individual and complete skeletons—littered the floor beneath the broken furniture. It was clear that a major battle had been fought here, a skirmish in the greater war for control of the temple. Various weapons lay scattered throughout, remnants of that violent confrontation.

Following a passageway to the south, Sequioa opened a door and immediately encountered several ghouls—mindless undead creatures with no allegiance to any temple, only to their insatiable hunger. Calmer confirmed what the party suspected: these creatures served no master but their own bellies. The ghouls attacked without hesitation, and the party responded in kind. Zinnia struck the first ghoul with brutal efficiency, while Sequioa killed one creature outright and wounded another. Zephyr, utilizing his assassin's training, dispatched two ghouls in rapid succession. One ghoul, sensing the tide turning against it, disengaged and fled into an adjacent chamber. The remaining ghouls pressed their attack, one charging Zephyr but missing, another striking Sequioa with claws and teeth, and a third lunging at Zinnia but failing to connect. Calmer invoked powerful magic, summoning spectral guardians that tore through the undead, and all three nearby ghouls succumbed to the divine assault.

The party pursued the fleeing ghoul into the adjacent chamber, but Zephyr's boldness led him into a trap. As he opened the door, seven more ghouls awaited within, their dead eyes gleaming with hunger. Zephyr charged forward, killing the wounded ghoul that had fled, but his aggressive positioning left him vulnerable. The ghouls swarmed him in a frenzy of claws and teeth, overwhelming his defenses and dragging him down into unconsciousness. More ghouls attacked Zinnia and Sequioa, tearing into them with savage fury, while another attempted to reach Calmer but failed to penetrate his defenses. Calmer's spectral guardians continued their work, devastating the ghouls that came within their reach. He then turned his attention to the fallen Zephyr, channeling healing magic that brought the rogue back from the brink of death.

The battle raged on with renewed intensity. Zinnia cut down multiple ghouls with her martial prowess, his attacks flowing from one target to the next with deadly efficiency. Sequioa struggled initially but eventually found his mark. Zephyr, revived and vengeful, struck down another ghoul with devastating force. The remaining ghouls fought desperately, one managing to wound Zephyr again before retreating from Calmer's spectral guardians, provoking a retaliatory strike. Another ghoul attacked Calmer directly, its claws and teeth finding purchase and breaking the cleric's concentration, causing his spectral guardians to dissipate. The loss of the powerful spell was a setback, but the party had already thinned the ghoul numbers significantly.

Calmer, wounded but determined, channeled healing magic into himself, restoring his strength. Zinnia and Sequioa finished off the remaining ghouls in the chamber, their weapons finding their marks with lethal precision. As the last ghoul fell, Zinnia's keen senses detected the sound of more undead approaching from the north. The party, bloodied but unbowed, made a crucial decision. Zephyr declared that they would continue fighting to conquer this territory for the greater glory of the Earth Temple, maintaining their cover while simultaneously clearing out the dungeon's threats.

The next wave of ghouls met a prepared and determined party. Zinnia struck first, killing a ghoul before it could fully enter the fray. Sequioa followed suit, his attacks becoming more confident and deadly as he cut down multiple undead. The ghouls that managed to close with the party found their attacks largely ineffective. Zephyr's daggers flashed in the dim light, dropping ghouls with precise strikes. Calmer, having learned from the previous encounter, invoked his spectral guardians once more, the divine magic tearing through the undead ranks. Zinnia and Sequioa worked in concert, eliminating the remaining ghouls with ruthless efficiency until the chamber fell silent once more.

With the immediate threat neutralized, the party turned their attention to the two ghasts they had encountered earlier. Calmer suggested a tactical approach, pre-casting his spectral guardians before engaging the creatures. He opened the door to the ghast chamber, and the undead threatened the party, declaring they would be nothing but food. Calmer, emboldened by their recent victories over twenty ghouls, retorted that the last ghouls had said the same thing. He invoked his spectral guardians, and the divine magic immediately began tearing into the ghasts. One of the creatures emitted a poisonous stench that overcame Calmer, weakening him, but the party pressed their advantage.

Zinnia entered the chamber and struck one of the ghasts, then tactically withdrew from the poisonous aura, though the movement provoked a retaliatory strike that wounded him. He resisted the creature's paralyzing touch through sheer fortitude. Sequioa charged the other ghast, his attacks finding their mark repeatedly until the creature fell. He then disengaged, avoiding the remaining ghast's stench. The surviving ghast attacked Calmer but missed, and Calmer's spectral guardians continued their relentless assault. Finally, Zephyr drew his pistol and fired, the shot echoing through the chamber as it struck the ghast and ended its unnatural existence.

The battle was over. The party stood victorious amid the corpses of a giant snake, over twenty ghouls, and two ghasts. They had successfully cleared an entire section of the dungeon, claiming new territory for the Earth Temple—or so it would appear to any observers. The party knew they would need to negotiate with Romag to officially claim this territory, and they learned that simply holding ground was not enough. They would need to recruit monsters to populate and defend their new domain, a task that would likely require future missions. For now, though, they had established themselves as formidable agents within the temple, positioning themselves perfectly to continue their dangerous mission of espionage and sabotage from within the heart of the enemy stronghold.

# Chapter 20 The Orb's Entrance

## Summary

The battle was over. The party stood victorious amid the corpses of giants and over twenty ghouls and ghasts, having successfully cleared an entire section of the dungeon. They had claimed new territory for the temple, positioning themselves as formidable agents within the enemy stronghold. However, they understood that simply holding ground was not enough—they would need to negotiate with Romag to officially claim this territory and recruit monsters to populate and defend their new domain.

The party approached Romag to report their success and lay claim to the cleared area. Romag, pleased with their accomplishment, invited them to sit and began an ideological training session. He explained that the Temple of Elemental Evil was founded on the principle that the true nature of the universe is constant chaos between the elements, with earth destined to reduce all things to dirt. The temple attracted those who embraced this inevitability rather than feared it, seeking to bring about the blessed moment when all would be reduced to a wasteland of earth.

When the party questioned why it was called the Temple of Elemental Evil rather than simply the Temple of Earth, Romag gritted his teeth in frustration. He explained that when the temple was founded, it was decided that the Earth Temple alone could not fight against all who would oppose it. The four elemental temples—earth, fire, water, and air—aligned to unify their ambitions and destroy their enemies, planning to settle their differences only after achieving victory.

Romag then revealed what had happened ten years ago. The forces of the temple had united and marched out to conquer, but they were defeated at the Battle of Emory Meadows by the Archbishop of Veluna and the Kingdom of Furyondy. The Circle of Eight, including powerful wizards like Bigby, Tenser, and Mordenkainen, had shown up and locked the temple itself, blocking access to the central driving power that gave them their necessary energy. Now they sat and plotted, searching for the key to unlocking the heart of the temple.

The party asked Romag about this key, and after considering their loyalty—having cleared territory, attended rituals, and proven themselves—he pulled out some brandy and shared it with them. He revealed that the key was an orb, the only object capable of forcing the chaotic elements to submit to the stability of earth. It was the shackle that binds the storm and flood, the tool to impose the discipline of stone upon the reckless factions of fire and water. Whoever held this orb would hold the mandate of the temple and have the power to supersede the bureaucratic authority of the greater temple.

Romag explained that with the orb, he could flood the dungeon with earth elementals and gorgons, crushing all opposing temples and ruling supreme. The orb was also the key to the vault that opened the deep places within the temple. When asked what was in the vault, Romag stated that such information needed to be earned—he did not yet trust them enough to reveal everything. The party understood they needed to continue proving themselves to learn more about the temple's deepest secrets.

To earn more trust, Romag tasked them with recruiting people to man the newly captured areas. Dick Rentsch had sent word of a group of professional mercenaries called the Broken Blades currently being recruited in Nulb. Romag was tired of relying on unreliable gnolls and unruly bugbears; he wanted disciplined, brutal soldiers loyal to gold. However, the Water Temple was also trying to recruit them, making it urgent for the party to secure the mercenaries first.

The party traveled to Nulb, arriving discreetly at the Watersteide Hostel, a run-down establishment with a sign reading "Rooms are cheap, lives cheaper." Inside the smoke-filled tavern room, they met with Dick Rentsch, who took them to his back room to discuss business privately. He explained that the captain of the Broken Blades, Dren Halveth, was currently taking bids and could be recruited in three ways: outbidding the Water Temple, defeating him in a fist fight, or completing a task for them. The party chose to pursue the option of completing a task.

Dick arranged a meeting with Dren Halveth, a half-orc with broad shoulders, scars, and one ruined white eye. His crew hung back behind him, relaxed but dangerous, with fingers drifting near weapons. Dren demanded to know who sent them, what they wanted in Nulb, and which gods they prayed to. The party explained they had gained territory in the temple for the Earth Temple. Dren revealed that a shipment of weapons belonging to his company had been stolen by the Crimson Reapers, who occupied the room next door. He tasked the party with retrieving the weapons without bringing the wrath of the temple down on everyone.

The party decided to try negotiating with the Crimson Reapers rather than stealing the chest. Calmer was sent to knock on their door while the rest of the party hid nearby. A tattooed and scarred Crimson Reaper opened the door and became angry when Calmer asked about the chest. When Calmer revealed he worked for the Earth Temple, the Reaper explained that the chest was temple property that Dren had stolen. He stated that Dren had until midnight to join the temple or he and his crew would be dead. The party realized the Crimson Reapers worked for a deeper organization behind the four elemental temples, not one of the four temples directly.

Returning to Dren, the party explained the situation: he must either join the temple or be killed by midnight. Dren expressed skepticism, noting the Water Temple was offering a large sum of money and claiming the Earth Temple just used recruits as cannon fodder. Sequoia countered brilliantly, arguing that the Water Temple relied on propaganda and lies while the Earth Temple showed real commitment by coming to recruit them personally. The party emphasized they had just claimed new territory and were willing to share it with the Broken Blades. Impressed by these arguments, Dren agreed to join the Earth Temple in exchange for one hundred gold pieces to help set up their new business.

As the party prepared to leave the hostel with the Broken Blades, Zinnia noticed that Wat, the barkeep, looked very unhappy about the outcome, confirming their suspicion that he worked for the Water Temple. When Calmer questioned Dick Rentsch about keeping Wat on his payroll despite his apparent loyalty to other factions, Dick responded that until they had secured complete power, everyone had to work together. As they departed, Wat approached Dren and whispered something to him, but Dren shoved him away.

The party successfully brought the Broken Blades back to the temple, where Romag was extremely pleased with their success. He announced that the new territory would be given to the mercenaries, who would report to the party, though he claimed another portion of the cleared territory for himself. That night, Romag hosted a celebratory drinking party in the newly cleaned banquet hall, which still carried the stench of cadavers and dead creatures mixed with food. Leaders from various areas of the temple attended the celebration. Romag proposed a toast to Sequoia, Zephyr, Zinnia, and Calmer for their contributions to increasing the Earth Temple's power, specifically highlighting Calmer's conversion from a cleric of St. Cuthbert to a follower of the elder elemental god. The party had successfully positioned themselves deeper within the temple's hierarchy, continuing their dangerous mission of espionage and sabotage from within the enemy stronghold.

# Chapter 21 The Otyugh's Maw and the Harpies' Call

## Summary

The morning dawned with Dren and his Broken Blades mercenaries settling into their newly claimed territory within the Earth Temple. Romag, pleased with the party's previous successes, suggested they continue their expansion by exploring and clearing the areas to the south and east. The party agreed, eager to further solidify their position within the temple's hierarchy and expand Romag's power base.

Their exploration led them through a passageway to an old wine cellar, where the stale reek of spilled beer, wine, and ale hung heavy in the air. The floor was covered with dark residue, and broken barrels, casks, and kegs lay strewn everywhere. Among the debris, they discovered one small intact barrel. When one of the party members smelled its contents and took a sip, they immediately regretted it, barely managing to spit out the disgusting liquid and avoid being poisoned, though they dry heaved for several minutes afterward.

Moving on, they found an adjacent storage chamber that had long fallen into disuse. Sacks of moldering grain and barrels of rotting flour sat among heaps of broken containers and spilled contents in a jumbled mess. The party continued their systematic exploration, determined to map out every corner of Romag's domain.

Their next discovery proved far more significant. They came upon a door secured with both a lock and a padlock holding a chain in place. Sequioa skillfully picked the lock, and the door swung open to reveal a filthy prison chamber. Inside, they found seven prisoners—two females and five males—all scantily clad, impoverished, and clearly suffering. The males were chained to the wall, and all of them looked terrified upon seeing the party's Earth Temple insignia.

The prisoners explained their circumstances with trembling voices. Most were local merchants who had been captured by raiders, but one stood out—Jorin Pike Kale, a river sailor who had been imprisoned by Dick Rentsch's men as punishment for making a pass at a woman named Dalia.
