They revealed the grim truth that Romag used them either to feed his gnolls or as sacrifices to the Earth Temple. Among the captives were Master Aldous Glimmerfield and Mistress Lenora Glimmerfield, textile merchants from Divers, along with their household staff and four farm boys turned mercenaries—Kalen, Brask, Horgus, and Davine—who had surrendered almost immediately when captured.

The party faced a difficult decision. They wanted to free the prisoners, but doing so would certainly anger Romag if he discovered their involvement. They devised a clever plan to frame the Fire Temple for the escape, using insignia and robes they had previously collected from Larith the Beautiful. However, they also had to contend with Dren's crew in the adjacent territory, who might hear the commotion. They decided to have Calmer stand guard at the door while the others freed the prisoners and scattered Fire Temple materials throughout the chamber.

Sequioa unlocked the prisoners' chains while the others kept watch. Zephyr then explored further and discovered a stairway leading up to an escape route. The party guided the grateful prisoners to freedom, and Jorin Pike Kale thanked them profusely. In gratitude, he shared valuable information about Captain Tolubb's pirate ship schedule at the docks in Nulb—knowledge that could prove useful given that Tolubb had previously betrayed the party by directing them into a Fire Temple ambush. The Glimmerfields promised a reward of one hundred silver pieces for their rescue.

The party decided to continue exploring before reporting the prisoner escape to Romag, hoping to give the freed captives a significant head start. They discovered several empty armories filled with wooden racks, broken weapons, sundered shields, ruined mail, and scattered skeletons of both humans and gnolls. Cobwebs hung in the corners, suggesting these areas hadn't been visited frequently. In one armory, they found five magical arrows that still retained their enchantment. As they explored, they began to realize that Romag, for all his bluster and ambition, had left large chunks of the dungeon empty and unused—a sign of either poor management or insufficient resources.

Their exploration led them to a passageway that descended to the second level of the dungeon, an area they had previously visited when meeting with representatives of the Air Temple. They recognized that this passage connected somewhere near the mysterious door that concealed an eldritch creature, though they chose not to venture down at this time.

Instead, they continued their systematic clearing of the current level and came upon a room that reeked of filth. The chamber was filled with trash, waste, and bones—its former purpose impossible to determine beneath the accumulated refuse. Zephyr attempted to convince Calmer to investigate the trash pile, arguing that the cleric had more health and could better withstand any dangers. Calmer, however, insisted they go together, pointing out that losing the party's healer would be far more catastrophic than Zephyr seemed to realize.

As they approached the refuse pile, a massive tentacled creature suddenly emerged from the garbage. Zinnia and Sequioa immediately recognized it as an Otyugh, a disgusting aberration that fed on waste and carrion. This particular specimen had clearly been sustained by the garbage generated by Romag's crew, but it viewed the party as far more appetizing than its usual fare. The creature's telepathic voice echoed in their minds, muttering "fresh meat" and "rot, good, do eat" as it prepared to attack.

The battle erupted with Zinnia striking first, landing solid blows against the creature's grotesque form. The Otyugh retaliated by lashing out with its tentacles, grappling both Zinnia and Zephyr in its powerful grip. It dragged Zinnia toward its gaping maw and bit down hard, injecting his with poison that sapped his strength and reduced his vitality. Sequioa unleashed a devastating series of attacks, his weapons finding purchase in the creature's flesh again and again.

Despite being poisoned and weakened, Zinnia managed to wrench himself free from the Otyugh's grasp and attempted to retreat to safety. However, the creature struck him one more time as she fled, and she collapsed unconscious. Zephyr, still grappled by the tentacles, continued to fight from his constrained position. Calmer, clearly annoyed by the situation and Zephyr's earlier insistence that the healer should go first, nevertheless performed his duty and channeled healing energy into Zinnia, bringing him back from the brink of death. He then strategically positioned himself away from immediate danger.

The Otyugh, frustrated by its prey's resilience, slammed its tentacles against both Zephyr and Sequioa, stunning them both with the force of the impact. Zinnia, recovered but still suffering from the poison coursing through his veins, delivered a series of strikes that finally brought the massive creature down. As it died, Calmer remarked pointedly about the importance of the healer not being in the front lines and cut Zephyr free from the dead creature's tentacles.

Following Calmer's suggestion, they searched through the disgusting refuse for anything of value. Their efforts were rewarded with the discovery of one hundred eighty silver pieces, fifteen gold pieces, and a glass vial containing a potion of greater healing that had somehow survived intact inside the creature's body, though it was sticky and smelled terrible. More disturbing was their discovery of a rusted, bent breastplate bearing the symbol of the Fire Temple. The implication was clear and chilling—Romag sometimes fed his rivals to this creature, using it as a living disposal system for his enemies. The party realized that Romag might not be pleased to discover they had killed his pet monster.

Undeterred, they pressed on with their exploration, entering a new chamber. As they stepped inside, they heard an ominous click, and a gate suddenly dropped behind them, sealing them in the room. Before they could react to this trap, they heard the sound of singing—beautiful, haunting, and utterly compelling. Harpies emerged from the shadows, their grotesque forms combining human-like features with the talons and wings of avian scavengers.

The creatures' notorious song filled the chamber, its magical compulsion washing over the party. Sequioa, succumbs to the enchantment, his minds clouded by the harpies' charm. Zephyr realizes that this is the song of only one of the harpies. And that although immune to it's song, the other two could charm him and the rest of the party.  The party realized desperate situation they now faced—trapped in a sealed chamber under threat from the harpies' magical influence, that could compel them to follow the creatures heedless of any danger that might await their victims. 

# Chapter 22 Harpies, Ghouls, and Earth Temple Rules

## Summary

The battle began with Zephyr launching attacks against a harpy that had charmed Sequioa, striking the creature multiple times before Zinnia joined the fray and delivered the killing blow that freed Sequioa from the enchantment. But the respite was brief, as two more harpies positioned themselves high in the rafters and began their haunting songs. The melodies washed over the party like a wave, and one by one they succumbed—Sequioa, Calmer, and Zephyr all fell under the charm, their eyes glazing over as the magic took hold. Only Zinnia remained free, her will strong enough to resist the supernatural compulsion.

With his companions entranced, Zinnia made the difficult decision to strike his own allies to break the enchantment. He attacked Zephyr first, his blow snapping him back to reality, then turned to Sequioa and finally Calmer, each strike accompanied by the hope that it would free them from the harpies' control. As the party regained their senses, three ghouls burst through the southern door, their rotting forms adding to the chaos of the battle. The undead creatures attacked with claws and teeth, one managing to paralyze Zephyr with its supernatural touch despite his initial resistance.

The harpies swooped down from their perches to attack Calmer, but the cleric proved a difficult target. As one harpy attempted to flee, Calmer seized the opportunity and struck it with his mace of smiting, the weapon connecting with devastating force in a critical blow. Throughout the escalating battle, Calmer proved invaluable as a healer, channeling divine energy to restore his wounded companions. Sequioa fought with remarkable ferocity, cutting down ghouls with powerful strikes, while Zinnia moved through the battlefield with deadly precision.

The situation grew more dire when four additional ghouls emerged from the northern entrance, and the harpies' songs once again threatened to overwhelm the party's minds. Zinnia found herself beset by multiple ghouls, their claws raking across her as she stood her ground. One ghoul managed to paralyze Calmer with its touch, leaving the cleric temporarily helpless, but he shook off the effect through sheer determination. The party worked together to systematically eliminate their foes, with Sequioa delivering particularly devastating blows that left enemies crumpled on the ground.

As the battle neared its conclusion, only a single harpy remained, perched in the rafters and snarling defiantly at the party below. Zephyr fired his pistol into the air in an attempt to frighten it away, but the creature attacked in response before attempting to flee. Both Zephyr and Zinnia struck at the escaping harpy, and Calmer called upon divine flame to scorch it. Finally, Sequioa took aim with a ranged weapon and brought the creature down from the rafters, ending the threat once and for all.

With the battle won, the party explored the rooms that had served as the harpies' lair. The first chamber reeked of death and decay, bones scattered across the floor and old cloth marking where the creatures had nested. Among the refuse, they discovered valuable elven chain mail hanging from the wall, though they decided to save it for later sale since Calmer already wore superior armor. In another abandoned room, they found a broken chest that Sequioa carefully examined before opening. Zephyr tried to get Calmer to open it, and the Cleric said no. Then he pushed Sequoia to open the chest, and the rogue agreed. Inside lay a modest treasure of gold, platinum, silver, and copper coins, which Sequioa distributed among the party—though he cleverly pocketed a slightly larger share for himself, Calmer and Zinnia, without Zephyr noticing.

Their exploration led them to an antechamber with plastered walls covered in obscene paintings and the remnants of once-fine furnishings. When they opened the next door, they encountered two ghasts who immediately challenged their presence, claiming the territory as their own. The undead creatures boasted of seizing the area from previous occupants, one even crunching on a human bone to emphasize their dominance. When threatened with violence, the ghasts proposed negotiation instead, and the party seized the opportunity to claim they worked for the Earth Temple.

The ghasts' reaction was immediate and enthusiastic—they declared their allegiance to the Earth Temple with fervent shouts, though it was clear to the more perceptive party members that the creatures were merely playing along for their own survival. Nevertheless, the party accepted this arrangement, effectively claiming the ghasts' territory as a subsidiary holding under the Earth Temple's banner. After taking time to rest and recover from their battles, they returned to Romag's office to report their success.

Romag sat in his office looking pleased as the party informed him of their territorial expansion and the defeat of the ghouls and harpies that had long refused to bow to his authority. He complained that ever since his predecessor had gone to work for the Deep Temple, the undead refused to work for him. The Earth Temple leader launched into a passionate speech about the power of the earth to crush all things to dust, praising the party for weakening the ghasts and bringing them under the temple's control. When the party asked about payment for their efforts, Romag explained that the arrangement worked differently than they expected—they were to bring treasure to the temple and pay him a portion, not receive payment themselves.

The conversation took a significant turn when the party expressed their desire for more power, official titles, and higher ranks within the Earth Temple hierarchy. Romag considered this request and agreed, but stated that such advancement would require a demonstration of loyalty. He instructed his subordinate Harch to prepare a ceremony, and when Zephyr nervously told Calmer to be ready to die, the cleric pulled him aside to express his growing discomfort with the situation. Romag clarified that the party members were too valuable to sacrifice—instead, prisoners would be used for the ritual, which would involve ripping their bodies apart as an offering to the earthen god. The session ended with Romag postponing the ceremony until the following day, though the party knew that the prisoners they had previously freed would be discovered missing, potentially disrupting Romag's plans and forcing them to blame the Fire Temple for the disappearance.

## Memorable Moments

**The entire party except Zinnia becomes charmed by singing harpies, forcing Zinnia to attack his own allies to break the enchantment.**

*Two harpies sang from thirty-four feet in the air, charming Sequioa, Calmer, and Zephyr while Zinnia remained free*

**Calmer lands a critical hit with his mace of smiting against a fleeing harpy, dealing ten points of damage.**

*Using his reaction as the harpy attempted to fly away after attacking him*

**Sequioa delivers a devastating blow to a harpy, dealing twenty-four points of damage and killing it instantly.**

*Four points off from his theoretical maximum damage, taking care of business*

**Sequioa successfully pockets extra treasure from the chest without Zephyr noticing, keeping a larger share for himself, Calmer, and Zinnia.**

*After finding treasure in a broken chest, Sequioa rolled a twenty-six on stealth while Zephyr failed his insight check*

"Hail the Earth Temple\!" — Ghast

*The ghasts immediately and enthusiastically declare allegiance to the Earth Temple when the party claims to work for it, though they are clearly just playing along*

## Scenes

### Harpy and Ghoul Ambush

#### The party engages in a chaotic battle against harpies who charm several party members, followed by ghouls emerging from a southern door.

- Zephyr attacks a harpy that had charmed Sequioa, dealing nine damage with the first attack and eight damage with the second.  
- Zinnia attacks the same harpy and kills it with eighteen points of damage, freeing Sequioa from the charm.  
- Two harpies positioned thirty-four feet up in the air begin singing, attempting to charm the entire party.  
- Sequioa, Calmer, and Zephyr all fail their saving throws and become charmed, while Zinnia successfully resists with a roll of eighteen.  
- Zinnia attacks Zephyr to break the charm, dealing six damage and allowing him to reroll his saving throw, which he passes with a twenty.  
- Zinnia attacks Sequioa, dealing eleven points of damage, allowing him to reroll and break free from the charm with a nineteen.  
- Zinnia attacks Calmer, dealing twelve points of damage to break his charm.  
- Three ghouls enter the combat from the southern door.  
- Zephyr attempts to attack a ghoul with his pistol but misses.  
- One ghoul attacks Zephyr with a claw, hitting for three points of slashing damage, but Zephyr resists paralysis with a seventeen on his constitution save.  
- A second ghoul attacks Zephyr with another claw, hitting and paralyzing him after he fails his constitution save.  
- The harpies swoop down to attack Calmer but miss, and one flies away.  
- Calmer uses his reaction to strike the fleeing harpy with his mace of smiting, dealing nine points of damage.  
- Calmer heals Sequioa for nineteen hit points.  
- Sequioa attacks a ghoul, dealing twenty-one points of damage and killing it, then misses his second attack.  
- Zinnia attacks a ghoul, hitting and dealing twelve points of damage, but it survives with thirteen hit points remaining.

### Paralysis and Reinforcements

#### Zephyr struggles with paralysis while the party battles waves of ghouls and harpies, with Calmer providing crucial healing.

- Zephyr fails his constitution saving throw and remains paralyzed.  
- A second ghoul rushes into combat and attacks Sequioa with a claw but misses.  
- Another ghoul bites the paralyzed Zephyr, hitting and dealing seven points of damage, three of which is necrotic.  
- The harpies swoop down to attack Calmer; one misses and flies away.  
- Calmer uses his reaction to strike the fleeing harpy with his mace, rolling a critical hit and dealing ten points of damage.  
- Another harpy attacks Calmer, hitting and dealing four points of damage.  
- Calmer heals Zephyr for twenty-three hit points.  
- Sequioa attacks a ghoul, hitting and dealing fourteen points of damage, killing it.  
- Sequioa attacks another ghoul, hitting and dealing fifteen points of damage.  
- Four more ghouls enter from the north, opening the door and joining the combat.

### Turning the Tide

#### The party begins to gain the upper hand, defeating ghouls and harpies despite Zephyr's continued paralysis.

- Zinnia attacks a ghoul, killing it with his strike.  
- Zinnia uses a key point for a bonus attack, dealing twelve points of damage to another ghoul.  
- Zephyr fails another constitution saving throw and remains paralyzed.  
- Two ghouls attack Zinnia with claws, hitting twice and dealing a total of nineteen points of damage.  
- A ghoul charges at Calmer and attacks with a claw but misses.  
- Another ghoul attacks Calmer, hitting and dealing five points of damage, and Calmer fails his constitution save and becomes paralyzed.  
- A harpy flies down and attacks Zephyr, hitting and dealing four points of damage.  
- Sequioa takes an attack of opportunity against a fleeing harpy, dealing six points of damage.  
- Another harpy attacks the paralyzed Zephyr but misses completely.  
- Calmer makes his saving throw and is no longer paralyzed.

### Final Confrontation

#### The party systematically eliminates the remaining harpies and ghouls, with Sequioa delivering devastating blows.

- Sequioa attacks a harpy, hitting and dealing twenty-four points of damage, killing it instantly.  
- Sequioa attacks another ghoul, hitting and dealing five points of damage.  
- Sequioa attacks again, hitting and dealing ten points of damage, killing the ghoul.  
- Zinnia attacks a ghoul, hitting and dealing ten points of damage, killing it.  
- Zephyr is no longer paralyzed and attacks a ghoul with sneak attack, dealing seven damage plus additional dice damage, reducing it to three hit points.  
- Zephyr makes a third attack, hitting and dealing seven damage, killing the ghoul.  
- A ghoul moves to attack Calmer but misses.  
- Another ghoul attempts to paralyze a party member but misses.  
- A harpy attacks Zephyr and misses, then attempts to fly away.  
- Zephyr uses his reaction to attack the fleeing harpy with his dagger, dealing six points of damage.  
- Calmer uses Sacred Flame on a ghoul, dealing nine points of damage.  
- Sequioa attacks a harpy, hitting and dealing nineteen points of damage, killing it.  
- Sequioa attacks another creature, hitting twice and dealing twelve points of damage total.  
- Zinnia attacks a ghoul, hitting and dealing seventeen points of damage.  
- The last harpy snarls at the party from the rafters, refusing to engage.  
- Zephyr shoots his pistol into the air to scare the harpy, which snarls back.  
- Zephyr attacks the harpy, dealing six points of damage.  
- The harpy attacks Zephyr and misses, then attempts to fly away.  
- Zinnia takes an attack of opportunity, dealing eleven points of damage.  
- Sequioa takes an attack of opportunity, dealing six points of damage, reducing the harpy to four hit points.  
- Calmer uses Sacred Flame on the harpy, dealing five points of damage.  
- Sequioa uses a ranged weapon to attack the harpy in the rafters, dealing ten points of damage and killing it.

### Exploring the Harpy's Lair

#### After the battle, the party explores rooms to the south, discovering treasure and valuable armor.

- The party decides to explore a door to the south of the Harpy Hall.  
- They enter a room reeking of carrion and filth, with bones littering the floor and old cloth indicating a nesting area.  
- The party discovers elven chain mail hanging from pegs on the wall.  
- The party discusses the properties of the elven chain mail, which provides a bonus to armor class and allows the wearer to be considered trained with it.  
- The party decides to keep the elven chain mail in their inventory for potential sale, as Calmer already has superior armor.  
- The party proceeds to another abandoned room littered with rags and bones, containing a broken chest in the northwest corner.  
- Calmer refuses to check the chest, stating he is not a rogue, and instructs Sequioa to open it.  
- Sequioa checks the chest for traps with a roll of twenty-eight and finds none.  
- Sequioa opens the chest and attempts to use stealth to pocket extra treasure, rolling a twenty-six.  
- Zephyr fails to notice Sequioa's deception with his insight check.  
- The chest contains thirty-seven gold pieces, seven platinum pieces, ninety-two silver pieces, and one hundred eight copper pieces.  
- Sequioa distributes the treasure, giving himself and Zinnia a slightly larger share than Zephyr.

### Negotiating with the Ghasts

#### The party encounters two ghasts in an antechamber and negotiates a temporary alliance under the guise of the Earth Temple.

- The party enters an antechamber with plastered walls covered in obscene paintings and remnants of upholstered furnishings.  
- The party opens a door and encounters two ghasts who question their presence.  
- The ghasts claim the territory as their own, stating they seized it from previous occupants.  
- A party member asks the ghasts what they are doing, and the ghasts respond that this is their territory.  
- The ghasts reveal they seized the territory from a human or elf, picking up a gnawed bone to demonstrate.  
- One ghast crunches on the bone and confirms it was human.  
- A party member threatens to kill the ghasts for killing someone, asking for their last words.  
- The ghasts propose arriving at a deal instead of fighting.  
- A party member asks if the ghasts will work under them, and the ghasts ask who the party works for.  
- The party claims to work for the Earth Temple.  
- The ghasts immediately declare allegiance to the Earth Temple, shouting 'Hail the Earth Temple\!'  
- An insight check reveals the ghasts are willing to go along with the fiction of being members of the Earth Temple for the moment.  
- The party agrees to this arrangement, effectively claiming the ghasts' territory as a subsidiary of the Earth Temple.

### Reporting to Romag

#### The party returns to Romag to report their territorial gains, leading to discussions about payment and a planned loyalty ritual.

- The party decides to make it official that they own the newly explored territory.  
- The party asks when they will start getting money for the territory.  
- The party decides to take a short rest before returning to Romag.  
- The party returns to Romag's office, where he is sitting and looking very pleased.  
- The party informs Romag that they have been expanding their territory to the southwest and want to put an official stamp on it.  
- Romag confirms that the party dealt with the ghasts, ghouls, and harpies that had refused to bow down to him.  
- Romag delivers a five-minute tirade about the power of the earth and how it crushes everything down to dust.  
- The party asks Romag how they get paid for their efforts.  
- Romag explains that the party is expected to bring treasure to the temple and pay him a portion, not the other way around.  
- The party expresses a desire for more power, official titles, and higher ranks within the Earth Temple.  
- Romag considers the idea and agrees, but states they need to see an act of loyalty first.  
- Romag instructs Harch to prepare a ceremony.  
- Zephyr tells Calmer to be ready to die.  
- Calmer pulls Zephyr aside and expresses that he is getting tired of the situation and reminds Zephyr that he also has an opinion.  
- Zephyr suggests that if Calmer dies, they would resuscitate him.  
- Romag overhears and clarifies that the party members are too valuable to be killed, and that prisoners will be used for the ritual instead.  
- Romag instructs Harch to prepare the prisoners for the ritual and expects the party to be active participants.  
- Calmer looks at the other party members, feeling nauseous, and asks if they are really going to go through with this.  
- Romag describes the ritual as an Earth Temple ceremony where they will rip the prisoners' bodies apart.  
- Calmer expresses his discomfort to Zephyr, Sequioa, and Zinnia.  
- Romag postpones the ceremony until tomorrow, instructing the party to rest.  
- The DM reminds the party that they freed the prisoners earlier and asks if they made a contingency plan.  
- The party confirms they planned to blame the Fire Temple for the missing prisoners.

## Locations

### Harpy Hall

A large chamber with high ceilings and rafters thirty-four feet up, where the party fought harpies and ghouls. The harpies positioned themselves in the air to sing their charming songs.

### Southern Room (Harpy Nesting Area)

A foul-smelling room reeking of carrion and filth, with bones littering the floor and heaps of old cloth along the walls indicating where harpies once nested. Old garments hang from pegs on the wall, and elven chain mail was discovered here.

### Abandoned Room with Broken Chest

A noisome place littered with rags and bones, featuring a broken chest in the northwest corner that contained treasure. A door to the west is located near the west corner.

### Antechamber of a Minor Functionary

An inner room with plastered walls covered in obscene paintings, containing the remains of upholstered furnishings including a couch and four chairs. Battered pewter dishes are heaped upon a small table. This is where the party encountered two ghasts who claimed the territory as their own.

### Romag's Office

The place where Romag conducts his business for the Earth Temple. The party reports their progress here and discusses their future within the temple hierarchy.

## NPCs

### Harpies

Winged creatures that use enchanting songs to charm adventurers. They position themselves thirty-four feet up in the air and attack with claws. When their plans are thwarted, they become furious and swoop down to attack party members.

### Ghouls

Undead creatures that attack with claws and bites, possessing the ability to paralyze their targets with a DC 10 constitution save. They have an armor class of twelve. Three initially emerged from a southern door, and four more later entered from the north. One ghoul initially focused on eating a dead harpy before joining combat.

### Calmer

A cleric and party member who wields a mace of smiting and uses Sacred Flame in battle. He provides crucial healing during combat, restoring nineteen and twenty-three hit points to party members. He expresses discomfort and weariness with Zephyr's decisions and is deeply troubled by Romag's planned ritual involving prisoners.

### Ghasts

Two territorial undead creatures encountered in an antechamber with obscene paintings. They claim to have seized the territory from a human or elf, demonstrating this by crunching on a gnawed bone. They feign allegiance to the Earth Temple when confronted by the party, though an insight check reveals they are merely playing along for their own benefit.

### Romag

The leader of the Earth Temple who sits in his office looking very pleased with the party's territorial expansion. He delivers passionate tirades about the power of the earth crushing everything to dust. He operates on a system where the party brings treasure to the temple and pays him a portion rather than being paid directly. He proposes a loyalty ritual involving prisoners where bodies will be ripped apart, and instructs Harch to prepare for this ceremony.

### Harch

An individual instructed by Romag to prepare prisoners for a loyalty ritual ceremony.

## Items

### Mace of Smiting

A powerful magical mace wielded by Calmer, used effectively to strike at flying harpies as they attempt to escape. Calmer landed a critical hit with it, dealing ten points of damage to a harpy, and also dealt nine points of damage on another strike.

### Pistol

A ranged weapon used by Zephyr, treated as an improved crossbow that deals 1d10 worth of damage rather than 1d6 points of damage. Zephyr used it to attack ghouls and shot it into the air to scare a harpy.

### Dagger

A melee weapon used by Zephyr for close-quarters attacks and as a reaction weapon. He used it to strike a fleeing harpy, dealing six points of damage.

### Elven Chain Mail

A piece of magical armor discovered hanging from pegs on the wall in the harpy nesting area. It provides a plus one bonus to armor class and allows the wearer to be considered trained with the armor even without proficiency in medium or heavy armor. The party decided to keep it in their inventory for potential sale since Calmer already has superior plate armor.

### Gold Pieces

Currency found in a broken chest. Thirty-seven gold pieces were discovered and distributed among the party, with some members receiving eight gold pieces and others receiving ten.

### Platinum Pieces

Currency found in a broken chest. Seven platinum pieces were discovered and distributed among the party, with most members receiving two platinum pieces and one member receiving three.

### Silver Pieces

Currency found in a broken chest. Ninety-two silver pieces were discovered and distributed among the party.

### Copper Pieces

Currency found in a broken chest. One hundred eight copper pieces were discovered and distributed among the party.

## Spells

### Sacred Flame

A divine spell used by Calmer to deal radiant damage to enemies. He used it against a ghoul, dealing nine points of damage, and against a harpy, dealing five points of damage. The targets must make a dexterity saving throw to avoid the damage.

# Chapter 23 The Chimera's Midnight Call

Date: Mar 11th, 2026

## Summary

The party's attempt at a long rest was shattered in the dead of night when Zinnia's sharp senses caught the unmistakable sound of dragging chains and a haunting, animalistic chorus echoing from beyond their southern door — the guttural roar of a lion twisted together with the frantic, deranged bleating of a giant goat. He roused his companions just in time, for moments later the door exploded inward as a monstrous Chimera came crashing through, its three heads snarling with demonic fury. Behind it, a Fire Temple warlock gripped the beast's chain and bellowed a declaration of war, proclaiming that Alrrem, the all-consuming, had sent his champion to break their bones and leave nothing but meat for the earth. The creature was a horrifying amalgamation — the hindquarters of a great goat, the powerful forequarters of a lion, and the leathery wings of a dragon, each head driven by its own savage instinct to hunt, hoard, and destroy.

The battle that followed was desperate and brutal. Sequioa charged the beast head-on but was quickly overwhelmed, battered into unconsciousness by the combined fury of the Chimera's attacks and the warlock's crackling magical blasts. Calmer called upon divine power to mend Sequioa's wounds and bring him back to his feet, even as the warlock continued to goad the beast forward. It was Zephyr who turned the tide, slipping past the rampaging monster and ripping the control chain clean from the warlock's hands, leaving the man screaming in terror at what he had unleashed. The Chimera, now free of its handler, thrashed wildly — its chain whipping through the air as the warlock scrambled desperately to reclaim it.

With the beast unbound, chaos reigned. The Chimera unleashed a devastating breath of fire upon both the warlock and a nearby bugbear who had tried to intervene, scorching them badly. Zinnia darted in to deliver a series of precise strikes before retreating to safety, while Zephyr managed to snatch up the fallen chain once more in a bold attempt to dominate the creature. The warlock, badly burned and barely standing, screamed that they were all mad — but Zinnia silenced him permanently with a flurry of devastating blows, leaving the Fire Temple's champion dead on the stone floor. The Chimera, however, was far from finished, and it turned its full, unhinged fury upon those who remained.

Zephyr bore the brunt of the beast's wrath, suffering a savage onslaught of bites and claws that left the rogue crumpled and unconscious. Calmer, muttering something about having only walking band-aids to offer, called upon his divine gifts once more to pull Zephyr back from the edge of death. Sequioa, having recovered and hidden himself behind a chest, loosed a precise shot that left the Chimera teetering on the very brink of collapse — but the enraged beast charged him before he could finish it, knocking him unconscious with a series of brutal strikes. It was Calmer who ended it, invoking the tolling resonance of necrotic power that rang out like a funeral bell, dropping the Chimera dead upon the cold stone floor.

In the quiet aftermath, the party searched the warlock's charred remains and uncovered a trove of revealing items. A wax-sealed ledger bore the crest of a crimson ship — a bill of sale from Captain Varsik of the Crimson Trader, detailing the purchase of the Chimera paid in full by the Fire Temple. More damning still, the name Vesrek  appeared in the document — an ally of Belsornig, the prophet of the Water Temple — suggesting the Water Temple had orchestrated the whole affair as a calculated win-win, either eliminating the party or bankrupting the Fire Temple in the process. The warlock also carried a heavy gold amulet bearing the eight-pointed symbol of the Fire Temple, a potion of fire resistance he had never managed to reach for in the chaos, and a masterwork whip of alchemically treated red leather. The heavy iron collar and chains used to control the beast were stamped with the marks of Nulb's River Pirates, revealing that someone in Nulb had been paid to deliver the monster to the temple.

The party brought their findings to Romag, who received the news of the chimera attack with thunderous fury, declaring that the moment to strike against Alrrem had arrived and ordering the party to deal with the Fire Temple's weakened leader. But it was a private meeting with Hartsch, Romag's sub-general, that proved far more illuminating. Hartsch laid out the political landscape of the temples in stark detail — Alrrem's power had been waning since the catastrophic defeat at the Battle of Emridy Meadows a decade prior, and the loss of his werewolves, followed by the ruinous cost of the chimera, had left him nearly broken. The Air Temple under the prophet Kelno was dismissed outright as a farcical remnant, reduced to a handful of rooms and no meaningful power. The Water Temple, however, remained a formidable rival, and Hartsch made clear that its elimination would be the true prize.

It was during this meeting that the party's ambitions crystallized into a bold and dangerous plan. Zinnia, using carefully chosen words and a disarming manner, drew out Hartsch's true feelings about Romag's passive, erosion-like style of leadership — and Hartsch made no effort to hide his belief in the destructive, earthquake-like power of the earth, nor his desire to wield it. When Zinnia pressed further, Hartsch replied with thinly veiled intent: if a position of leadership were to suddenly open itself, he would absolutely consider himself worthy of it. The party recognized the signal for what it was — Hartsch wanted Romag removed, and he wanted them to do it. With a plan taking shape to assassinate Romag, install Hartsch as the Earth Temple's new leader, and ultimately engineer a three-way war between the Earth, Fire, and Water temples to destroy all three factions from within, the party quietly agreed that the time for subtlety was nearly over.

## Memorable Moments

**Zephyr slips past the rampaging Chimera and rips the control chain from the Fire Temple warlock's hands, sending the beast into an uncontrolled frenzy.**

*A bold and decisive move that turned the tide of the fight, leaving the warlock screaming in terror at what had been unleashed.*

"I have walking band-aids. That's what I have." — Calmer

*Exasperated after being asked yet again to heal a downed party member mid-combat, having already revived multiple companions.*

"I would always, if a position were to suddenly have opened itself, I would absolutely like to think that my faith and my leadership qualities would be considered." — Hartsch

*Hartsch's thinly veiled response to Zinnia's deceptive probing, subtly signaling he wants the party to assassinate Romag.*

## Scenes

### A Rude Awakening

#### While the party attempts to take a long rest, Zinnia hears strange noises outside their door, leading to a sudden and violent ambush.

- Zinnia wakes the party after hearing the sound of dragging chains and animalistic roars outside their door.  
- A Chimera, controlled by a warlock from the Fire Temple, smashes through the door to attack the party.  
- The warlock commands the beast to tear the party apart as retribution for their actions against the Fire Temple.  
- Sequioa rushes the Chimera but is quickly overwhelmed by the beast's ferocity and the warlock's magical blasts.  
- Calmer uses divine magic to harm the Chimera and heal the fallen Sequioa.  
- Zephyr maneuvers past the beast to confront the warlock directly, successfully ripping the control chain from his hands.  
- The warlock is left terrified as he loses control of the monstrous Chimera.

### The Chimera's Rampage

#### A chaotic battle ensues as the party attempts to neutralize a Chimera that has broken free from its Fire Temple handler.

- Zinnia strikes the Chimera and retreats to a safe distance.  
- The Chimera breaks free from its chain, terrifying its warlock handler.  
- A bugbear attempts to distract the Chimera by pointing a weapon at it, but the beast unleashes a devastating breath attack on both the warlock and the bugbear.  
- Zephyr successfully snatches the dragging chain from the ground while remaining stealthy.  
- Zinnia delivers a lethal blow to the Fire Temple warlock before turning his attention back to the Chimera.  
- The Chimera focuses its fury on Zephyr, unleashing a flurry of bites and claw attacks that leave the rogue unconscious.  
- Calmer uses divine magic to heal Zephyr, bringing him back into the fight.  
- Zephyr attempts to use the chain to intimidate and dominate the beast, but the Chimera thwarts the effort.  
- Sequioa hides behind a chest and launches a precise ranged attack, leaving the Chimera on the brink of death.  
- The Chimera charges Sequioa, knocking the halfling unconscious with a series of brutal strikes.  
- Calmer invokes necrotic power, finally slaying the Chimera as it stands over the fallen party members.

### Aftermath and Looting

#### The party takes a short rest to recover and searches the fallen warlock and chimera for valuables.

- The party takes a short rest to recover from the intense combat.  
- Zinnia observes Calmer's behavior, questioning whether the cleric is genuinely embracing his Earth Temple cover identity or simply acting.  
- The party loots the fallen warlock, discovering a ledger bearing a wax-sealed receipt from Captain Varsik of the Crimson Trader detailing the purchase of the chimera.  
- A heavy gold amulet depicting the eight-pointed symbol of the Fire Temple is found on the warlock.  
- A potion of fire resistance is recovered, which the warlock had apparently intended to use but never reached for in the chaos.  
- A masterwork whip woven with alchemically treated red leather is found among the warlock's belongings.  
- The heavy iron collar and dragging chains used to control the beast are found to be stamped with the marks of Nulb's River Pirates, revealing that someone in Nulb was paid to bring the monster to the temple.

### Reporting to Romag

#### The party informs Romag of the Fire Temple's failed assassination attempt and discusses their next move.

- The party meets with Romag and Hartsch to report the chimera attack.  
- Romag expresses outrage over the Fire Temple's audacity and the theft of prisoners.  
- The party explains that the Fire Temple is financially overextended after purchasing the chimera.  
- Romag orders the party to deal with Alrrem, the leader of the Fire Temple, declaring that the moment to strike is now.  
- The party begins to consult with Hartsch about the layout of the temple and Alrrem's location.

### Subverting the Earth Temple

#### The party meets privately with Hartsch to gauge his loyalty and ambition, and begins plotting to overthrow Romag.

- The party moves to their private quarters to meet with Hartsch away from Romag's ears.  
- Hartsch reveals the history of Alrrem and the Fire Temple's recent financial struggles following the loss of their werewolves and the chimera.  
- Hartsch explains that Vesrek , an ally of Belsornig the prophet of the Water Temple, was the one who arranged the chimera purchase — suggesting the Water Temple manipulated the situation as a win-win.  
- Hartsch dismisses the Air Temple and its prophet Kelno as a joke with very little remaining power or territory.  
- The party proposes a plan to trigger a three-way war between the Earth, Fire, and Water temples to weaken all factions.  
- Zinnia uses deception to suggest to Hartsch that he would be a superior leader to Romag.  
- Hartsch expresses his belief in the 'vengeance power of the earth' and a preference for a destructive, earthquake-like leadership style over Romag's slow erosion approach.  
- Hartsch implies he would be willing to take command if a position were to 'suddenly open itself', signaling to the party that he wants them to remove Romag.  
- The party decides to move forward with a plan to kill Romag and allow Hartsch to take over, believing he will be more aggressive in attacking the other temples.

## NPCs

### Fire Temple Warlock

A servant of Alrrem who led a Chimera on a chain to assassinate the party. He used magical blasts and commanded the beast until Zephyr disarmed him of the chain. He was ultimately slain by Zinnia.

### Chimera

A horrific multi-headed monstrosity with the features of a lion, a goat, and a dragon, imported by the Fire Temple at great expense to destroy the party. It went on a mindless rampage after being freed from its chains and was ultimately slain by Calmer's necrotic magic.

### Bugbear

A guard or observer who attempted to intervene in the chaos by pointing a weapon at the Chimera, but was caught in the beast's devastating breath attack.

### Calmer

A cleric and party member who healed fallen companions and ultimately slew the Chimera. His companions grew concerned that he may be genuinely embracing his Earth Temple cover identity rather than simply acting.

### Romag

The leader of the Earth Temple who prefers to lead from behind, viewing himself as a chess king who sends others to fight his battles. He ordered the party to deal with Alrrem after hearing of the chimera attack.

### Hartsch

Romag's sub-general and speaker who provided the party with tactical information and maps. He revealed the political landscape of the temples and subtly signaled his desire for the party to remove Romag from power so he could take command.

### Alrrem

The leader of the Fire Temple, currently weakened after losing his werewolves and his expensive chimera asset. He was originally the leader of the temples in the great battle of Emridy Meadows and has been struggling to hold power since.

### Belsornig

The Prophet and head of the Water Temple, suspected by Hartsch of being a cunning manipulator who arranged the chimera purchase through his ally Vesrek  as a win-win scheme against both the Fire and Earth temples.

### Kelno

The Prophet of the Air Temple, dismissed by Hartsch as a joke with very little remaining power or territory, reduced to a few rooms within the temple.

### Vesrek

An ally of Belsornig, the Water Temple prophet, who is identified in the ledger as the one who arranged the purchase of the chimera for the Fire Temple through Captain Varsik of the Crimson Trader.

## Locations

### Party's Quarters

The room where the party attempted to take a long rest, featuring a southern door and a secret door. The Chimera smashed through the door here, leading to a desperate struggle, and the room later served as a private meeting place with Hartsch.

### Romag's Chambers

The command center for the Earth Temple where Romag and Hartsch coordinate their forces. The party reported the chimera attack here and received orders to deal with Alrrem.

## Items

### Chimera Chain

A heavy iron collar and dragging chains used by the Fire Temple warlock to control and goad the Chimera into battle. Zephyr managed to rip the chain from the warlock's hands during the fight. The chains were later found to be stamped with the marks of Nulb's River Pirates, revealing that someone in Nulb was paid to deliver the monster.

### Warlock's Ledger

A document found on the Fire Temple warlock, containing a heavy wax-sealed receipt bearing the crest of a crimson ship. It is a bill of sale from Captain Varsik of the Crimson Trader detailing the purchase of the chimera, paid in full by the Fire Temple. It also revealed a connection to Vesrek , an ally of the Water Temple's prophet Belsornig.

A heavy gold amulet and chain depicting the eight-pointed symbol of the Fire Temple, found on the warlock. Its craftsmanship and gold weight make it worth 100 gold pieces.

### Potion of Fire Resistance

A magical elixir found on the warlock, intended to protect the user from heat and flames. The warlock had it on his person but panicked and never reached for it during the chaos of the chimera's rampage.

A high-quality whip woven with alchemically treated red leather, found among the warlock's belongings. While not magical, it is of fine craftsmanship, features a ten-foot reach, and can slow an enemy's movement.

## Spells

Beams of crackling energy fired by the Fire Temple warlock at Sequioa, dealing significant damage early in the fight.

### Healing Word

A quick divine incantation used by Calmer on two separate occasions during the battle — first to restore Sequioa to consciousness, and later to bring Zephyr back from the brink of death.

### Toll the Dead

A necrotic spell cast by Calmer that manifests as the sound of a melancholy tolling bell. It was used multiple times during the fight and ultimately delivered the final, fatal blow to the wounded Chimera.

# Chapter 24 Temples Ablaze: The Three-Way War Begins

Date: Mar 26th, 2026

## Summary

The night had barely settled before the party found themselves deep in the aftermath of a brutal battle. A Fire Temple warlock had unleashed a monstrous Chimera upon them — a horrifying amalgamation of lion, goat, and dragon — proclaiming it the champion of Alrrem, the all-consuming. The beast had ravaged the party, battering Sequioa and Zephyr into unconsciousness before Calmer's divine gifts pulled them back from the brink of death. In the end, it was Calmer's necrotic resonance, ringing out like a funeral bell, that dropped the Chimera dead upon the cold stone floor.

In the quiet aftermath, the party searched the warlock's charred remains and uncovered a trove of damning evidence. A wax-sealed ledger bore the crest of a crimson ship — a bill of sale from Captain Vesrek of the Crimson Traitor — detailing the purchase of the Chimera by the Fire Temple. More revealing still, the name Belsornig appeared in the document, an ally of the Water Temple, suggesting that the Water Temple's prophet had orchestrated the entire affair as a calculated win-win: either eliminate the party or bankrupt the Fire Temple in the process. The warlock also carried a heavy gold amulet bearing the eight-pointed symbol of the Fire Temple, a potion of fire resistance he had never managed to reach for, a masterwork whip of alchemically treated red leather, and heavy iron chains stamped with the marks of Nulb's river pirates.

The party brought their findings to Romag, the High Priest of the Earth Temple, who received the news with thunderous fury and ordered them to strike the weakened Fire Temple. But it was a private meeting with Hartsch, Romag's sub-general, that proved far more illuminating. Hartsch laid out the political landscape in stark detail: Alrrem's power had been waning since a catastrophic defeat a decade prior, the Air Temple was a powerless remnant, and the Water Temple remained the true rival. When Zinnia pressed Hartsch on his feelings about Romag's passive, erosion-like philosophy of leadership, Hartsch made no effort to hide his ambitions — if a position of leadership were to suddenly open itself, he would absolutely consider himself worthy of it.

The party recognized the signal for what it was. Hartsch wanted Romag removed, and he wanted them to do it. A bold and dangerous plan crystallized: assassinate Romag, install Hartsch as the Earth Temple's new leader, and engineer a three-way war between the Earth, Fire, and Water Temples to destroy all three factions from within. They quietly agreed that the time for subtlety was nearly over — but not quite yet.

Rather than alerting Hartsch to their intentions, the party chose to execute a surgical coup without his foreknowledge, planning to present him with power as a fait accompli. They enlisted Dren, the leader of the Crimson Guard, to arrange a morning meeting with Romag, ensuring the high priest would be present and off-guard. Dren, visibly impressed by the party's victory over the Chimera, agreed without hesitation and returned with confirmation that Romag was looking forward to the meeting.

The party slipped through a secret passage into the Earth Temple's inner sanctum, emerging into Romag's sleeping chamber — a room decorated with earthen motifs, its floor covered in dirt, a small altar in the corner and a desk scattered with personal notes. Sequioa crept behind a curtain separating the chamber from the adjoining room, where he could hear Romag lecturing Hartsch on the slow, erosive power of dust, while Hartsch barely concealed his disdain behind carefully chosen words of agreement. Sequioa also noticed, with some urgency, that the door to the south stood open — if Romag cried out, guards would come flooding in and the entire operation would unravel.

The party moved swiftly. Zephyr invoked a subtle but powerful magic, causing the doors of Romag's chamber to slam shut with a resonant bang, sealing the room from the outside world. Hartsch, instantly understanding what was happening, placed his weapons on the ground and stepped aside. Romag turned, his face shifting from confusion to horror as Zinnia rushed him with a flurry of precise, devastating strikes, followed by Sequioa landing blow after blow on the stunned high priest. Calmer, nearly invoking the name of his holy patron before catching himself in the most spectacularly ill-timed moment imaginable, instead channeled a wave of necrotic power that caused Romag to wither and collapse dead upon the cold stone floor.

Hartsch did not hesitate. He plunged his hands into Romag's body, covered them in blood, and marched into the main chamber of the Earth Temple, holding Romag's sacred vestments aloft. He declared himself Supreme Prophet, announcing that the era of passive resignation was over and that the destructive power of earth would now be unleashed upon their enemies. The gathered cultists erupted in cheers, and Hartsch introduced the party to his troops as the Obsidian Edge — his personal vanguard, the sharpest stone in the deep earth, cutting through fire and water from the shadows before anyone felt the quake.

With Hartsch rallying his forces, the party slipped away to set the second part of their plan in motion. They descended to the second level of the dungeon, passing through a magically lit corridor pulsing with gray, green, and red light, its massive brass doors covered in leering faces and horrible scenes. At the entrance to the Fire Temple, Zephyr slipped a note under the door claiming to have information about a betrayal. When Alrrem's voice finally came through the door, the party expressed admiration for the power he had unleashed and revealed the truth: Belsornig of the Water Temple had orchestrated the Chimera incident, using the Fire Temple as a pawn to eliminate the party or drain Alrrem's resources. The silence that followed was brief — then Alrrem's enraged voice screamed Belsornig's name and ordered his troops to attack the Water Temple immediately.

The party vanished before the Fire Temple doors could open and made their way back to Hartsch, who was deep in ritual and speechifying, summoning earth elementals and whipping his cultists into a frenzy. They were welcomed back as heroes, publicly hailed as the Obsidian Edge before the assembled army. When the party informed Hartsch that the Fire Temple was now fully distracted by its assault on the Water Temple, Hartsch erupted with triumphant fury and ordered a full-scale charge. The war had begun.

As Hartsch's forces surged forward, the party followed just long enough to maintain appearances before peeling away. They encountered Dren preparing his men for battle, and after a brief exchange, sent him and his Crimson Guard into the fray. But the advance quickly stalled — a lieutenant reported that Fire Temple bugbears had dragged heavy wooden pews and stone rubble across the stairway to the second level, creating a makeshift barricade that was bottlenecking the entire Earth Temple assault. Hartsch turned to the Obsidian Edge and asked them to do what his army could not.

The party chose to bypass the barricade entirely by cutting through the Air Temple, a section of the dungeon that was eerily, unsettlingly quiet. They knew a wraith haunted its halls, and sure enough, the spectral guardian began drifting toward them as they moved through the dim corridors. Rather than engage it, the party ran, and the wraith, seemingly satisfied once they had fled its domain, turned back into the darkness. Calmer, catching his breath, noted with grim amusement that the Air Temple's leader Kelno was so ignorant he apparently believed the wraith to be an air elemental — a fitting symbol of the faction's irrelevance.

Emerging onto the second level, the party found a chaotic battlefield flooded with water and choked with zones of scalding steam, where Fire and Water Temple forces were already tearing into each other. Dead bodies from both factions littered the ground. Through the haze, they could see the Fire Temple bugbears holding the barricade against the Earth Temple's stalled advance. The party prepared to strike, coordinating their assault to hit the bugbears from behind and break the stalemate. Zinnia moved first, rushing the lead Fire Temple warrior through the mist with a pair of precise dagger strikes — and the battle for the second level had begun in earnest.

## Memorable Moments

**Hartsch plunges his hands into Romag's body, covers them in blood, and marches into the main chamber to declare himself Supreme Prophet of the Earth Temple.**

*Immediately after the assassination, Hartsch seizes power in a visceral, theatrical display that confirms the party's coup has succeeded.*

"For Saint Cuthbert— ...I have learnt that it is not erosion that is the power of Earth, but its destructive power." — Calmer

*Calmer nearly invokes his holy patron mid-assassination before catching himself, realizing it would be the worst possible thing to say in an evil temple.*

**The party slips a note under the Fire Temple's door and, through tense negotiation, convinces Alrrem that Belsornig betrayed him—triggering the temple war with a single conversation.**

*A masterclass in manipulation: the party instigates a three-way war between the temples without drawing a single weapon.*

## Scenes

### The Chimera's Night Attack

#### A recap of the previous night's brutal battle against a Chimera sent by the Fire Temple, which shattered the party's long rest.

- Zinnia hears the sound of dragging chains and animalistic roars outside the party's door in the dead of night.  
- A Fire Temple warlock unleashes a Chimera—a beast with the hindquarters of a goat, the forequarters of a lion, and the wings of a dragon—proclaiming it a champion of Alrrem sent to destroy the party.  
- Sequioa charges the beast but is quickly knocked unconscious by its combined fury and the warlock's magical blasts.  
- Calmer uses divine power to mend Sequioa's wounds and bring him back to his feet.  
- Zephyr slips past the rampaging monster and rips the control chain from the warlock's hands, causing the Chimera to turn its fire breath upon its former master and a nearby bugbear.  
- Zinnia delivers a series of precise strikes to the warlock, eventually killing him.  
- The Chimera savagely attacks Zephyr, leaving him unconscious before Calmer heals him.  
- Sequioa nearly collapses the beast with a precise shot before being knocked out again by the enraged Chimera.  
- Calmer ends the battle by invoking a necrotic tolling resonance that drops the Chimera dead on the cold stone floor.

### Evidence and Alliances

#### The party investigates the aftermath of the battle, uncovers damning evidence, and plots a political coup within the Earth Temple.

- The party loots the warlock's charred remains, finding a wax-sealed ledger linking the Chimera's purchase to Captain Vesrek of the Crimson Traitor and revealing the Water Temple's prophet Belsornig as the orchestrator.  
- The party also discovers a heavy gold Fire Temple amulet, a potion of fire resistance the warlock never reached for, and a masterwork alchemically treated red leather whip.  
- The iron collar and chains used to control the Chimera are found to be stamped with the marks of Nulb's river pirates.  
- The party brings their findings to Romag, who orders them to strike the weakened Fire Temple.  
- A private meeting with Hartsch, Romag's sub-general, reveals the political landscape: Alrrem is nearly broken, the Air Temple is a powerless remnant, and the Water Temple is the true rival.  
- Zinnia draws out Hartsch's true feelings about Romag's passive leadership, and Hartsch makes clear he would consider himself worthy of leadership if a position were to open.  
- The party formulates a bold plan: assassinate Romag, install Hartsch as the Earth Temple's new leader, and engineer a three-way war between the Earth, Fire, and Water Temples to destroy all three factions from within.

### Planning the Coup

#### The party discusses their strategy to assassinate Romag and install Hartsch as the new leader of the Earth Temple.

- The party decides to perform a surgical strike against Romag without informing Hartsch beforehand to maintain leverage.  
- Zephyr reasons that killing the boss in a cultist hierarchy means the killer is assumed to be the new boss, so the party plans to immediately pledge loyalty to Hartsch afterward.  
- The party enlists Dren, leader of the Crimson Guard, to set up a morning meeting with Romag to ensure the high priest is present and off-guard.  
- Dren, impressed by the party's victory over the Chimera, agrees to facilitate the meeting.

### Infiltration of the Earth Temple

#### The party sneaks through a secret passage into the high priest's quarters to eavesdrop on Romag before the assassination.

- The party navigates a secret hallway leading into the Earth Temple's inner sanctum, using magic to open the secret door silently.  
- They enter a sleeping chamber decorated with earthen motifs, dirt-covered floors, a small local altar, and a desk with personal notes.  
- Sequioa sneaks behind a curtain to eavesdrop on a conversation between Romag and Hartsch in the adjoining room.  
- Romag lectures Hartsch on the 'slow, erosive power of dust' while Hartsch barely conceals his disdain for the passive philosophy.  
- Sequioa notices that the door to the south is open, which could allow guards to rush in, creating an urgent need to seal it before the assassination begins.  
- The party coordinates a plan: Zephyr will use magic to slam the doors shut, isolating Romag and Hartsch from the guards, before the others rush in.

### The Assassination of Romag

#### The party executes a surgical coup in Romag's private chambers, ending his reign and installing Hartsch as the new leader of the Earth Temple.

- Zephyr uses Thaumaturgy to slam the doors of Romag's chamber shut, isolating the high priest from his guards.  
- Hartsch immediately recognizes what is happening, surrenders his weapons, and stands down.  
- Romag, stunned and unable to call for help in time, is rushed by Zinnia, who delivers a series of devastating strikes.  
- Sequioa joins the assault, landing precise blows against the stunned high priest.  
- Calmer invokes a necrotic resonance like a tolling funeral bell, causing Romag to wither and die.  
- Hartsch marks his hands with Romag's blood and marches into the main chamber, declaring himself Supreme Prophet and announcing a new era of destructive power.

### The Rise of the Obsidian Edge

#### Hartsch rallies the Earth Temple troops and introduces the party as his elite vanguard, the Obsidian Edge.

- Hartsch presents Romag's religious vestments to the gathered cultists, announcing that the era of passive resignation is over.  
- The party adopts the moniker 'The Obsidian Edge' as Hartsch's personal agents of chaos.  
- Hartsch introduces the party to the assembled troops, hailing them as the architects of the coming victory.  
- The party maneuvers Hartsch into launching an immediate offensive against the Fire Temple while it is to be distracted by the Water Temple.

### Instigating the Temple War

#### The party travels to the second level to manipulate the Fire Temple into attacking the Water Temple, then returns to trigger the Earth Temple's invasion.

- The party slips away from the cheering Earth Temple cultists and encounters Dren, who is preparing his men for war.  
- The party tells Dren to join the fight, and he rallies his men with promises of plunder and blood.  
- The party descends to the second level, passing through a magically lit corridor with brass doors and a massive bronze portcullis.  
- Zephyr slips a note under the Fire Temple's door claiming to have information about a betrayal.  
- The party negotiates with Alrrem through the door, expressing admiration for his power and revealing that Belsornig of the Water Temple orchestrated the Chimera incident to bankrupt him.  
- Enraged by the news of the betrayal, Alrrem orders his troops to immediately attack the Water Temple.  
- The party returns to the Earth Temple, where Hartsch is performing a ritual and giving speeches about destruction.  
- The party is publicly hailed as the 'Obsidian Edge' by Hartsch in front of his gathered troops.  
- The party informs Hartsch that the Fire Temple is now distracted by its attack on the Water Temple, prompting Hartsch to order a full-scale assault.

### The Bottleneck at the Barricade

#### As the three-way war erupts, the Earth Temple's advance is stalled by a Fire Temple barricade, and the party is called upon to break the stalemate.

- Hartsch leads his cultists and warriors out of the Earth Temple in a chaotic surge toward the other factions, with the party following to maintain their cover.  
- A lieutenant of Hartsch reports that Fire Temple bugbears have blocked the stairs to the second level with heavy wooden pews and stone rubble, creating a stalemate.  
- Hartsch requests the Obsidian Edge's help to break through by sneaking behind enemy lines.  
- The party decides to bypass the main bottleneck by traveling through the Air Temple.

### Infiltration Through the Air Temple

#### The party sneaks through the eerily quiet Air Temple, narrowly avoiding a spectral guardian.

- The party enters the Air Temple, which is eerily quiet and known to be haunted by a dangerous wraith.  
- A wraith begins to approach the group, but the party chooses to run as fast as possible rather than engage it.  
- The wraith pursues briefly before turning back as the party flees into the lower levels.  
- Calmer identifies the wraith and notes that the Air Temple's leader, Kelno, mistakenly believes it to be an air elemental, revealing the faction's ignorance and weakness.

### Assault on the Fire Temple Rearguard

#### The party reaches the second level war zone and launches a surprise attack on the bugbears guarding the barricade to allow the Earth Temple forces to break through.

- The party discovers a battlefield flooded with water and filled with zones of scalding steam where Fire and Water Temple forces are clashing, with dead bodies from both sides visible.  
- The party identifies the Fire Temple bugbears holding the barricade against the Earth Temple's main force.  
- The party prepares a coordinated strike against the bugbears and a Fire Temple leader holding the barricade.  
- Zinnia initiates combat by rushing the Fire Temple leader, striking with daggers through the hazy mist.  
- The party reaches a new level of power mid-skirmish, having gained experience from their previous victory over a temple leader.

## NPCs

### Alrrem

The leader of the Fire Temple, described as 'the all-consuming.' He sent the Chimera to destroy the party. When the party revealed that Belsornig of the Water Temple had orchestrated the Chimera incident to bankrupt him, he flew into a rage and immediately ordered his troops to attack the Water Temple.

### Hartsch

Romag's sub-general in the Earth Temple. He is ambitious and believes in the destructive power of earth over Romag's passive philosophy. He subtly encouraged the party to remove Romag, and after the assassination he declared himself Supreme Prophet, marked his hands with Romag's blood, and launched a full-scale war against the Fire Temple. He is a megalomaniac who views the party as his elite vanguard, the 'Obsidian Edge.'

### Romag

The former High Priest of the Earth Temple who advocated for a slow, erosive approach to power, believing dust to be the ultimate force. He was assassinated by the party in his own chambers after being isolated from his guards.

### Belsornig

The prophet of the Water Temple, whose name appeared in the warlock's ledger as the orchestrator of the Chimera purchase—a calculated move to either eliminate the party or bankrupt the Fire Temple.

### Captain Vesrek

Captain of the Crimson Traitor, mentioned in the wax-sealed ledger as the one who sold the Chimera to the Fire Temple on behalf of the Water Temple.

### Kelno

The prophet of the Air Temple, whose faction is dismissed as a powerless remnant. Calmer noted that Kelno is so ignorant he mistakes the wraith haunting his temple for an air elemental.

### Dren

Leader of the Crimson Guard faction and an ally to the party. He was enlisted to set up a morning meeting with Romag and later rallied his men to join the temple war after being promised plunder and blood.

### Calmer

A cleric of St. Cuthbert and member of the party. He healed fallen allies during the Chimera battle, delivered the killing blow to both the Chimera and Romag using necrotic power, and assisted in the infiltration despite being naturally noisy.

### Hartsch's Lieutenant

A commander in the Earth Temple army who reported to the party that the Fire Temple bugbears had blocked the stairs to the second level with a makeshift barricade, requesting the Obsidian Edge's help to break the stalemate.

### The Wraith

A spectral undead guardian haunting the Air Temple that briefly pursued the party before they fled into the lower levels. Kelno's cultists mistake it for an air elemental.

## Locations

### Earth Temple

The base of operations for the Earth Temple faction, where the party survived the Chimera attack, plotted and executed the coup against Romag, and where Hartsch rallied his troops by summoning earth elementals and delivering speeches about a new era of destructive power.

### High Priest's Sleeping Chamber

A room decorated with earthen motifs, dirt-covered floors, a small local altar, and a desk with personal notes. The party infiltrated this chamber via a secret passage and used it as a staging point for the assassination of Romag.

### Second Level Corridor

A twenty-foot-wide passageway pulsing with gray, green, and red magical light, featuring heavy brass doors covered in horrible scenes and leering faces, and a massive solid bronze portcullis controlled by a windlass.

### Fire Temple Entrance

A defensible position on the second level where the party slipped a note under the door and negotiated with Alrrem through it, revealing the Water Temple's betrayal and triggering the Fire Temple's attack on the Water Temple.

### The Air Temple

An eerily quiet section of the dungeon haunted by a wraith that the Air Temple's leader, Kelno, mistakes for an air elemental. The party passed through it quickly to bypass the barricade blocking the Earth Temple's advance.

### The Second Level War Zone

A chaotic battlefield flooded with water and filled with zones of scalding steam where Fire and Water Temple forces clashed. Dead bodies from both temples were visible, and Fire Temple bugbears held a makeshift barricade of pews and rubble against the Earth Temple's forces.

## Items

### Wax-sealed ledger

A document bearing the crest of a crimson ship, found on the warlock's charred remains. It detailed the purchase of the Chimera by the Fire Temple from Captain Vesrek of the Crimson Traitor, and crucially contained the name Belsornig—an ally of the Water Temple—revealing that the Water Temple had orchestrated the entire affair as a calculated win-win scheme.

### Eight-pointed Fire Temple amulet

A heavy gold amulet bearing the eight-pointed symbol of the Fire Temple, found on the dead warlock's remains, signifying his rank within the temple.

### Masterwork red leather whip

An alchemically treated whip of red leather, found on the warlock's remains. It was used to control the Chimera during the attack.

### Potion of fire resistance

A magical draught found on the warlock's charred remains. He never managed to reach for it during the chaos of the battle.

### Iron collar and chains

Heavy iron restraints used to lead and control the Chimera, stamped with the marks of Nulb's river pirates, revealing that someone in Nulb had been paid to deliver the monster to the temple.

### Romag's Vestments

The religious symbols and garments of the Earth Temple's high priest. After the assassination, Hartsch seized them and held them aloft before the gathered cultists to symbolize his new authority as Supreme Prophet.

## Spells

### Toll the Dead

A necrotic resonance that sounds like a tolling funeral bell, used by Calmer. It was used twice in this session: first to deliver the killing blow to the Chimera during the night attack, and again to wither Romag's body and end his life during the assassination.

### Guidance

A divine blessing cast by Calmer to bolster the party's competence during their stealthy infiltration, granting a bonus to skill checks such as Sequioa's eavesdropping attempt.

### Thaumaturgy

Cast by Zephyr to cause the unlocked doors of Romag's chamber to slam shut, sealing the room and preventing guards from intervening during the assassination.

### Guiding Bolt

Attempted by Calmer during the struggle against Romag before he switched to a different approach.


# Chapter 25  Opening Battles

---

## Zinnia — The Scalding Battlefield

The steam hit first.

Not the heat — the *steam*. That particular species of pain that gets inside your lungs before you've registered it's happened. We came up from the lower level into a corridor that had turned itself into something between a battle and a sauna, and my first thought wasn't *danger*, it was *assessment*. Stone floor. Bodies in Earth Temple brown and Fire Temple red, already cooling in the scalding air. Visibility: poor and narrowing. Bugbears behind an improvised barricade of pews and rubble, holding the stairway against an Earth Temple advance that had stalled somewhere in the haze ahead of us.

*Someone set up a very effective choke point.*

I almost appreciated it professionally, for about half a second.

Zephyr put an arrow into the mist from the edge of the corridor — precise, efficient, for once doing the sensible thing from distance. I noted it. Then I stopped noting things and started moving.

The cultist at the front of their line didn't see me coming. That's the point. Through the steam, into the billowing white heat, and then just — strikes, a sequence of controlled impacts, the kind of work where your body knows the geometry better than your mind does. The cultist dropped before the bugbears had finished processing that we were there. Clean. Fast. The momentum of the whole corridor shifted for one clear, perfect moment.

Then the bugbears reminded me what a barricade is for.

I've taken hits that hurt more. I've taken hits that did worse. But there's a specific quality to a critical blow — the way it doesn't just land on you, it *reorganizes* your understanding of how the fight is going. The blow took something real from what remained, landing on a body that had just committed fully to the attack and had nowhere to go. The sound it made wasn't something I want to think about.

I don't go down. That's not bravado; it's training. But I also don't pretend it didn't happen. I was aware of my own edges in a way I hadn't been thirty seconds before — the specific ache of ribs that had absorbed something they didn't enjoy absorbing, the adjustment that happens when your body starts disagreeing with your decisions. Zephyr and Sequoia had caught pieces of the same counterattack. I had caught the largest piece.

*You rushed in.* 

I know. I know I rushed in.

The thing is, the barricade was the whole problem, and the cultist at the front was the hinge of the barricade. If you take the hinge, the door opens. That's not impatience. That's geometry.

What broke it, in the end, wasn't geometry. It was Calmer.

A single pulse of guiding radiance, the kind of divine light that doesn't ask permission, and the double doors at the barricade simply *stopped existing as an obstacle*. They flew open with the kind of finality that makes everyone on both sides pause for half a breath. Then the Earth Temple forces came through the gap like the tide deciding to try something different, and the Fire Temple line fractured into the exact kind of chaotic mess that we had specifically engineered this situation to produce.

I watched Zephyr work through the first bugbear with a methodical efficiency that I don't say enough good things about. Sequoia's finishing blow on the second had a certain satisfying weight to it. The barricade was gone. The stalemate was gone. The careful hold the bugbears had maintained was just — gone.

Which left the steam.

The steam was still there, and it was getting worse. Elemental violence has a way of feeding itself, and whatever was generating this particular environmental nightmare was not winding down. The mission was accomplished in the narrowest operational sense: the barricade broken, the advance resumed, the fire and earth factions in the process of tearing into each other exactly as intended. We had done what we came here to do.

*Time to not be here anymore.*

There's a specific discipline in the disengagement — recognizing that the chaos you've set in motion no longer belongs to you and no longer cares who you are. You're not retreating from failure. You're exiting the kill zone you personally created before it becomes indiscriminate about its victims. That's just operational hygiene.

My ribs had opinions about how quickly we should move.

I moved quickly anyway.

---

## Calmer — Confrontation with the Wraith

I knew before I turned the corner. Not by sight or sound — by the particular wrongness that St. Cuthbert's training leaves in a cleric who has seen enough of what shouldn't exist. I came around the bend into the Air Temple passage and there it was, drifting in the dark ahead of us. Waiting. Patient in the way that things without hearts are patient.

My first instinct, if I'm being honest, was the same instinct I had the first time I ever came face to face with something that should not exist. Pure, animal want-to-be-elsewhere. Not fear exactly. Fear still leaves you room to answer back. This was the other thing — older than training, older than faith — the kind that gets in before St. Cuthbert can.

But I am a cleric of St. Cuthbert, and St. Cuthbert does not permit elsewhere as a destination when undead are in the corridor.

I gathered the light. I thrust it outward — the real thing, the genuine article, the power that has cracked ghoul skulls and sent lesser dead screaming into whatever passes for oblivion on the other side. I aimed it at the wraith with all the conviction I possess, which is considerable.

The wraith looked at it. And did not flinch. The holy light dissolved into the cold air like breath in winter, leaving exactly nothing behind.

There is a particular quality to failure when it happens in front of an audience. The wraith didn't flinch. The corridor didn't tremble. The darkness simply remained exactly as dark as it had been, and the wraith remained exactly as present, and I stood there holding the afterglow of a divine power that had achieved nothing at all.

A lesser cleric might have retreated at that point. A wiser one, perhaps. But I have been killed once already in this gods-forsaken temple, revived at considerable cost to someone who claims not to care about me, and I did not survive all of that to be mocked by something that doesn't even have the decency to be alive when it defies me. I tried again.

There was an abiding and pure passion in that second attempt — I will not dress it up as something nobler. It was hatred. I hate this thing. I hate what it is, what it represents, the cold contempt of its existence, the way it simply *drifts* while I channel power that has worked before and should work now.

The wraith held against it again.

Two attempts. Two failures. The divine light of St. Cuthbert, Cudgel of Ortho, the Arbiter Eternal, dispersed twice into the air of a dank dungeon corridor while an undead abomination stood untouched and completely unbothered.

*This is not fair*, I thought, which is not a thought with a great deal of theological dignity, but it was the honest one.

Then the wraith turned its hollow gaze on all of us, considering the room, and it said, with what I can only describe as serene confidence: *"Well, one of you is going to die today."*

Zephyr raised his pistol.
